## Failed And Interrupted Evidence

- Worker focused first run exposed benign authorization-enum summary redaction.
  The summary field name was corrected without weakening secret redaction.
- Frontend first run failed a widened TypeScript literal fixture; second run
  found the real route/context bug and an Advanced-only control expectation.
  Both failed logs remain; the third full run passed after scoped fixes.
- Recovery first run failed on a SELECT alias inside a COLLATE expression. A
  direct query on the owned synthetic database confirmed PostgreSQL's rejection.
  The canonical-row query now sorts an outer-query column binding.
- Docker subsequently disconnected during the backend full suite and second
  recovery run. Docker was not restarted by this phase. It later became reachable
  again. The four exact exited synthetic containers were individually validated
  against owner, full ID, daemon, image, configuration and stopped state, then
  removed. Their original manifests, media, partial bundles and uniquely named
  interrupted-cleanup receipts remain intact. No private containers/volumes or
  host evidence were deleted. No database-verification success is asserted for
  that interrupted cleanup.
- Native verification uses a temporary portable Go 1.25.13 Windows SDK, with the
  official archive SHA256 verified before extraction. No system/PATH installation.
  Archive, tool directory and test evidence are retained; dependency lookup is
  offline. This cannot substitute for Linux, real-store or installer acceptance.
- The first native IDP attempt found old password-change test tokens missing a
  role. Fixtures now use normally generated tokens with the matching stored role;
  production role validation was not weakened. The second complete suite passes.
- Real-store runner first failed before provisioning: Windows command discovery
  returned both Docker executable aliases. Parent selected the first application
  explicitly. Second fresh run passed the real tests and cleanup; first evidence
  remains intact.
- The first owned application image build found a misplaced parent-authored Go
  build argument in the provider-fixture stage. It was moved to the shared builder;
  the contract now verifies shared-stage scope. Failed build logs are preserved.
  Corrected current IDP/backend image builds subsequently passed. The frontend
  image build was deliberately cancelled for the user's resource-cleanup request;
  browser checks remain unexecuted, not failed or passed acceptance.
- An existing 67,090,158-byte `backend/.hai-backend-deploy` binary is preserved but
  excluded from future Docker contexts. This is packaging/resource hygiene, not
  measured 90% whole-system savings or deletion of the original artifact.

## User-Requested Resource Cleanup

The user redirected work to reducing local Docker/test-environment load. The
owned frontend image-build client was identity-checked and terminated; its
runner subsequently exited 1 with preserved logs. No new acceptance stack,
browser or Docker build/test run was started after the request. At that cleanup
checkpoint all eleven agents were closed. The later low-resource continuation
ran 80 local CI contracts successfully, integrated one source-only agent and
closed it. No agent remains open.

Two guarded cleanup receipts verified removal of 34 and then three exited HAI
Go test/build/format runners (37 total). Logs were saved before removal. Full
IDs, stopped state, image, command and mount guards were checked, and removal
used neither force nor volume deletion. Receipts and logs remain in private
temporary directories `hai-container-cleanup-318c8535376d49dc8485c53ebc740926`
and `hai-container-cleanup-ba0b398e19fd478a8688d71344cf36ee`.

Source/worktrees, database containers, volumes, images, caches and installation
containers are retained. The user explicitly requested that Joyce and ShareT
keep running. A Cofounder test database restarted during the cleanup and remains
outside this parent task's lifecycle ownership. Final running-container stats
showed no HAI executor, and process inspection showed no owned acceptance build
client. Removing already-exited containers does not itself reduce running CPU;
no measured speedup, RAM saving or 90-percent reduction is asserted.

## Low-Resource Source Handoff

The local CI contract suite passed 80 tests in 3.216 seconds with exit 0.
This is local contract proof, not an executed hosted workflow or live-store test.

Before entering the safe-worker broker, background execution now records an
uncertain attempt with its identity, artifact name, marker hash and optional
claim generation. A failed first result write therefore leaves a replay
restriction instead of an empty receipt. This is planned intent, not evidence
that permission was consumed, an artifact was created or execution succeeded.
Original world-model fields and exact JSON numbers are retained. Cancellation
before dispatch cannot invoke the executor.

Unclaimed `Transition` and `Save` require atomic operation-plus-audit persistence;
unsupported repositories fail closed. Gorm uses one transaction and memory one
lock, retaining version, owner/workspace and claim guards. At this earlier
checkpoint, Ingest creation still used a separate created-event append. The later
atomic intake section below addresses that source gap.
Nine fault-injection wrappers were adapted without disabling their failures.
The parent reviewed and formatted all affected Go sources.

The native six-package verification attempt was deliberately stopped before any
test events appeared, to avoid prolonged host work. It used one Go worker, a
384 MiB soft heap target, disabled dependency downloads and no Docker or live
accounts. The exact executable, PID 51588, parent PID 14952 and command were
checked before termination; the runner then exited 1 and reported Go exit -1.
`production-low-resource-integrated-20261001.jsonl` is retained as an empty
attempt log, not passing evidence. Compilation and newly added Go tests were
unverified at that checkpoint; later serial reruns below supersede that limit.
No private app or database was stopped or restarted in this handoff.
See [atomic mutation source report](operation-audit-atomicity-20261001.md).

## Authoritative State And Bounded Native Verification

The previous turn changed source and completed cleanup, so it was progress, not
a verified wait or a completed-product claim. This continuation preserved the
resource constraint: two source-only sidecars, no new Docker/test environments,
no downloads, and only one native Go verification process at a time. Both new
sidecars are now closed and their changes are integrated into this checkout.

Source review exposed an internal contract bug: keeping the current version but
inventing a Verifying before-status could complete a stored Ready operation.
This was not established as an external API exploit. Atomic unclaimed and claimed
mutations now check the locked/current before-status and revalidate the state
machine before any write. Non-transition Save cannot change status. Invalid
completion verification remains refused. Memory claimed writes reject duplicate
audit IDs before changing state or releasing a lease. Creation timestamps stay
immutable in memory and SQL-returned operation snapshots, matching the SQL CAS
helper's existing omission of created_at.

The parent added claimed/unclaimed state, lease retention, duplicate audit,
creation-time and synthetic SQL pre-write regressions. The background cancellation
regression also checks bounded finalization and retention of the replay barrier.
Source notes are retained separately; their unexecuted sidecar findings are not
being relabelled as agent-executed proof.

The verifier now has an overall deadline, separate stdout/stderr, a terminal
receipt, disabled dependency downloads and identity-checked cancellation of its
owned process tree. Default scope is one package. A six-package batch timed out
after 120 seconds before test events, and its receipt records failure. A child
termination warning was retained; subsequent process inspection found no remaining
Go/compiler/linker process. Smaller scopes completed serially; this is not a
measured whole-system 90-percent resource saving.

| `production-low-resource-locked-background-fixed-20261001.jsonl` | Exit 0; background/executionbroker, 76 top-level / 134 test-subtest passes, three skips, zero failures. Includes pre-dispatch intent, failed first-result write and cancellation cases. |

an existing test fixture. The fixture now explicitly creates an older Ready
record before the fresh intake, rather than depending on successive wall-clock

## Atomic Intake And Truthful Feed Synchronization

The cleanup turn changed authoritative container state and preserved Joyce/ShareT,
so it was progress. This continuation used two source-only sidecars and serial,
bounded native verification, with no new Docker stack, downloads, live provider,
private migration/restart, commit, push or merge.

`Service.Ingest` now normalizes owner/workspace consistently and validates complete
input plus evidence JSON before lookup or mutation. Opaque dedupe keys are retained.
New actions and their UUID-bound `created` events require atomic creation support;
Gorm inserts both in one transaction, memory under one mutex. Malformed pairs and
operation/event identity conflicts fail before partial memory writes. Caller inputs
are copied. Only the exact active-dedupe PostgreSQL unique violation, or its explicit
driver diagnostic, triggers collision reload; an audit error cannot masquerade as
that operation collision. Unknown commit outcomes return failure, not success.

Existing evidence refresh uses the atomic Save path with a `source_evidence_refreshed`
event. It preserves stored status, approvals, uncertainty receipts, world-model JSON
and creation chronology, and refuses stale snapshots or claimed actions. The review
found that a creation-race loser returned success without saving different evidence;
ordinary and collision duplicates now share the same refresh/claim fence. Identical
concurrent inputs still create one pair. The existing uncertainty test now provides
complete input without relaxing any no-retry or receipt assertions.

Feed item validation/storage errors now produce `sync_failed` or `sync_partial`, not
`synced`. Such reports withhold their cursor, so it cannot authorize skipping failed
records. Last attempt and last fully successful sync have separate timestamps;
failed/partial attempts do not advance successful freshness. The Angular account
bridges view displays them separately. Error messages stay bounded and generic.
The feed registry itself remains in-memory; this change does not claim durable
provider cursor storage, a native provider connector, or live account acceptance.

Includes eight simultaneous identical inputs, collision-refresh claim refusal, malformed/duplicate creation pairs and synthetic SQL operation/audit/commit faults. |
| `production-low-resource-atomic-intake-background-reviewed-20261001.jsonl` | Exit 0; background/executionbroker, 76 top-level / 134 test-subtest passes, three skips, zero failures. Receipt-preservation and no-retry assertions retained. |
| `production-low-resource-atomic-intake-consumers-reviewed-20261001.jsonl` | Exit 0; phase2/runtimelab/opscontrol, 108 top-level / 157 test-subtest passes, zero skips/failures. |
| `production-low-resource-atomic-intake-feeds-reviewed-20261001.jsonl` | Exit 0; accountfeed, 18 top-level / 28 test-subtest passes, zero skips/failures. Covers partial/all failure, idempotent retry, safe diagnostics and successful timestamp retention. |

The first background intake rerun failed at the sparse fixture; its log is retained

signed Windows release, or live provider result is implied.

## Focused Angular Verification And Feed Observation Audit

Two source-only sidecars ran; both are closed and their reports are incorporated
in this checkout. The parent owned edits, integration and executable checks.

The installed Angular builder reads all roots in its tsconfig even when test
entrypoints are selected with `--include`. Current `tsconfig.spec.json` has 77
spec roots. New `tsconfig.account-bridges.spec.json` narrows only the focused
compiler roots to one spec; it inherits all strict settings. The normal full-suite
config is unchanged. A new source contract checks both TypeScript and Angular
compiler inheritance and runs through the existing npm test command.

The test now imports the real AccountBridgesModule instead of reconstructing a
dynamic declaration scope. The first focused AOT attempt exposed missing-scope
template errors; the real-module correction passed 25 tests. After the UI review
fixes, the final AOT/template/native Chrome run passed 30. No schema suppression,
template/type-check disabling, dependency replacement or assertion removal was used.

UI changes distinguish never-loaded/unavailable totals from a verified empty
registry; keep last-known evidence and timestamp after refresh failure; block
single/bulk imports during loading or failed feed-status refresh; offer a refresh
recovery action; retain visible labels in accessible action names; and expose
separate UTC attempt/success times with original instants in HTML time attributes.
DOM regressions cover these states, zero reads, invalid dates, legacy fallback,
post-sync failure refresh and distinct instants across the repeated DST hour.

Backend changes reject missing/null/non-array envelope collections before exposing
cursor/freshness while preserving explicit empty arrays. Same-registry overlapping
sync calls are rejected before fetching/intake without falsifying last-attempt
state. Other feeds can continue independently. A deterministic blocked-intake
test verifies the later observation is accepted only after the earlier attempt
finishes and remains the latest evidence. Failure/recovery coverage now clears the
injected storage rejection and verifies two creation/audit pairs exactly once while
they remain active. This does not change intentional archived/dismissed re-intake.

## Durable Account-Feed Registry Continuation

The former production router created an in-memory registry on every start. It
now calls `NewPostgresRegistry`, probes both migrated tables, fails initialization
on unavailable storage, and propagates bootstrap failures. Migration
`pre/0110_account_feed_registry` is additive. Configured local feeds have stable
owner/workspace/path-bound IDs; re-registration returns existing configuration
without changing operator choices or generating duplicate registration audits.
No ephemeral fallback is available in the production composition.

The request-context API is explicitly owner/workspace scoped and error-returning.
The old no-error helpers are restricted to isolated memory fixtures; they panic
if used with a durable store rather than turning storage failures into successful
empty results. Register, patch, sync-start and sync-finish combine their state
change and audit insert in one PostgreSQL transaction. The sync claim is committed
before fetching or ingesting; a matching UUID token is required to finish. No
timer auto-clears a potentially live/interrupted writer. Finish storage failures
retain previously committed operations, leave the claim pending and return an
empty cursor plus `recorded: false`. This is not a transaction spanning the feed
and all operations or an exactly-once guarantee. Intentional archived/dismissed
operation re-intake remains unchanged.

The UI exposes running/interrupted imports even in Basic mode, rejects their
single/bulk controls, distinguishes unverified current read counts, refreshes
after an unconfirmed request, and treats an absent/false completion audit flag
as needing review. Enabled feeds are labelled as bulk-import participants, not
as a fabricated background schedule. Unknown sync states are fail-closed.

An integration-tagged PostgreSQL test is prepared with an exact, literal-loopback

UUID-named schema, creates that schema and the reviewed migration transactionally,
pins and verifies the namespace on every connection, and only registers cleanup
after setup commits. It has bounded pools, connection/statement/lock waits and a
45-second test context. Its separately bounded cleanup cannot erase preexisting
fixtures and uses schema `RESTRICT`, not `CASCADE`. It exercises
committed reads via a fresh pool, concurrent claims, stale writers, induced audit
failure rollback, owner/workspace exclusion and immutable audits. It has not
been executed. A verified operator recovery flow, actual crash/database restore
acceptance and production DML-role/migration acceptance remain required. Historical
volatile registrations already lost cannot be recreated by the migration.

## Source Safety And Contextual Intake Continuation

Local JSON intake now uses the existing rooted filesystem boundary, rejects path
links/substitution, checks regular-file identity after the bounded read, and
honors cancellation before opening and after reading. Both actual reader paths
use this boundary. New HTTP feed registrations reject URL credentials, fragments
and secret-looking decoded query keys; ordinary pagination keys are permitted.

Generic intake preserves the supplied RFC3339 timestamp (including subsecond
precision) as a UTC instant while retaining the original JSON evidence. Missing
dates remain missing. Provider/account identity survives normalization; a
structured versioned dedupe hash removes delimiter ambiguity, and normalized
title/type/time changes produce new source revisions. Registration scope and
project remain authoritative rather than trusting unreviewed item routing.

Historical v1 keys are not silently rewritten or copied into approved work. An
active old-key collision requires operator reconciliation and preserves its
record and audit trail. There is no implemented reconciliation interface yet.
The rollout must exclude mixed old/new writers: the compatibility lookup is not
an atomic two-key lock. Distinct new revisions do not automatically invalidate
already approved older revisions; that lifecycle remains a separate safety gate.

Due bulk import claims recheck `Enabled` under the repository lock before any
claim/audit mutation. An explicit manual import may still operate on a disabled
bulk participant. Contextual operation intake requires an explicit repository
capability and propagates the request context through lookup, transactions,
locks, CAS and audit writes. It does not silently use a context-free decorator.
Only recording the matching sync outcome uses separately bounded detached
cleanup; cancellation cannot be converted into a successful cursor advance.

### Final Review Amendments

The parent removed item-dependent hash-version selection from `ToOperationInput`.
Both reader paths now use the same v2 structured identity and always carry the
historical-key review fence. Explicit and implicit provider/account overrides
with the same effective identity cannot duplicate work; changing an item account
without a provider override still changes provenance. Equivalent timestamp
offsets normalize to the same UTC revision identity. Legacy test records are
seeded with their actual historical key explicitly, not by making the current
reader take an unsafe branch.

Public `Feed` and `SyncReport` JSON serialization redacts recognized URL userinfo,
credential query values, fragments, malformed URL material and secret-bearing
report errors/cursors. It works for direct records, nested health/list responses
and partial failure reports, without rewriting the persisted/fetch configuration.
Ordinary cursor values and scope isolation remain intact. This is bounded
redaction, not universal recognition of arbitrary secrets in unknown query names,
paths or opaque values. Historical credentials still require operator-managed
removal/rotation; serialization does not perform those mutations.

The audit reconfirmed that historical-key lookup and new creation are separate
transactions, and an existing canonical row takes precedence. No mixed-version
writer guarantee or full historical-source inventory is claimed. Quiesced
cutover, old/new coexistence reconciliation and older approved-revision lifecycle
acceptance are still required before release.

End-of-round verification: the two latest sidecars are closed, all parent-owned
verification sessions have terminal exit receipts, and focused Go formatting and
tracked-file whitespace checks pass. The Chrome component run emitted launcher
termination warnings after its 37 successful cases; subsequent process inventory
found no headless/Karma Chrome processes. HAI's Docker stack remains stopped;
Joyce and ShareT were preserved, and other projects' active test jobs were not
stopped or removed. No 90 percent resource saving, live deployment, full visual
acceptance or overall production completion is claimed.

## Read-Only Identity Inspection And Intake Result Guards

This subsequent low-resource phase adds a real authenticated GET route and a
typed inspector in the existing Account Bridges module. It does not replace
the original scope with inspection-only functionality or claim historical
reconciliation is finished.

### Implemented Contract

- `GET /api/v1/account-feeds/:id/identity-preview` uses the actual owner and read
  permission middleware, returns `Cache-Control: no-store`, and permits only
  configured local JSON sources. No provider fetch, sync claim, ledger mutation
  or audit append is performed by inspection.
- A rooted bounded regular-file read supplies at most 100 inspected items from
  the current file, with at most 200 canonical/historical ledger lookups and a
  ten-second child deadline. Scope, timestamp, observed/inspected totals,
  truncation and incomplete historical inventory are explicit. Labels have
  bounded recognized-secret redaction, not universal secret detection.
- Canonical and historical active keys are both observed. Results distinguish
  unseen, canonical, historical and coexisting identities. Archived/dismissed
  history is not inventoried; the result is not an atomic snapshot, migration
  authority, approval or execution grant.
- The registry is re-observed before returning. Observed configuration or
  attempt/success/count changes reject the report as `identity_preview_changed`;
  a sync token rejects it as busy. Late failures/cancellation discard accumulated
  results. This is two observations, not serialization against intervening writes.
- Frontend selection and refresh invalidate stale results. Changed-preview,
  busy, unsupported and safe unknown errors remain visible in Basic view;
  inspection cannot invoke synchronization or manufacture successful imports.
- Background readers use `IngestContext`; explicit contextual adapters are
  required. Fault decorators preserve cancellation and their injected failures
  without silently falling back to context-free methods.
- All intake lookup paths, including canonical, historical and creation-race
  reads, reject null/contradictory/inactive/wrong-scope/wrong-key results. Create
  and refresh responses must also match the expected ID/version. Expected
  creation identity is captured before handing a pointer to persistence. A
  malformed successful write response is uncertainty, not rollback proof or
  authority to retry blindly.
- Memory and PostgreSQL operation lists, due queues and dashboard recency have
  deterministic ID tie-breaks. Negative pagination offsets are clamped. The
  memory due queue now excludes future review dates, matching the SQL filter.

### Remaining Source-Head Safety Finding

Source review confirmed that a v2 source revision changes the dedupe key and
creates another operation, without invalidating the old operation. The old safe
operation can still reach local artifact creation after newer information was
ingested. The subsequent prerequisite described below now binds the exact locked
running operation/version/live claim and source tuple/revision in authorization;
it does not establish authoritative current semantic-source generation.
Human-approved external execution was not proven in this inspected safe-worker
chain and must not be inferred from this finding.

The remaining fix requires a durable server-resolved source head serialized with
intake through the final-effect boundary. An unlocked latest-timestamp check is
insufficient. Structured provider/account/external identity must not be
reconstructed by ambiguous source-URI string parsing. Replayed or out-of-order
older revisions must not silently promote an old head. Intake/effect locking
order must be consistent across replicas, old approvals/history retained, and
meaningful changes require renewed approval where applicable. Background,
phase2, direct broker, runtime-lab and host-tool entry points need review and
actual concurrency/crash/network-failure acceptance. The source-head fence is
not implemented or accepted, and no exactly-once claim is made.

## Durable Structured Source Identity Foundation

This subsequent phase persists exact provider/account/external record fields and
a versioned SHA-256 digest without parsing colon-delimited display URIs. It
preserves meaningful bytes, validates UTF-8/control characters and byte bounds,
and requires a valid revision for identified operations. Revision and owner scope
are deliberately not part of the semantic digest; repository lookup remains
owner/workspace scoped. Different revisions may coexist without sharing dedupe.

Canonical intake validates stored provenance against the normalized input. A
historical all-empty identity requires reconciliation, not a silent backfill.
Ordinary, audited and claimed repository mutation paths reject changes to the
identity tuple/digest or identified revision. Safe execution validates malformed
identity before dispatch, and persisted-state mutation checks reject caller-only
provenance substitution. This is not an authoritative current-source effect fence.

Migration `pre/0111_operation_source_identity` adds grouped fields, a scoped
nonunique index and an immutability trigger. Historical rows remain unchanged.
Its rollback locks writers and refuses schema removal while any identity fields
are populated. SQL checks are structural; they do not recompute the digest or
claim exact parity with all application Unicode validation. No personal database
was migrated. A real PostgreSQL upgrade/immutability/rollback test is prepared and
compiled, but it requires an explicitly owned isolated database and remains
unexecuted. The native runner explicitly disables destructive database tests.

| `production-low-resource-source-identity-consumers-final-20261001.log` and receipt | Terminal 0, no timeout; complete executionbroker/phase2/opscontrol/runtimelab suites after the identity changes. Synthetic/local proof, not real providers or a full repository run. |
| `production-low-resource-source-identity-router-final-20261001.log` and receipt | Terminal 0, no timeout; integration-tagged selected `Test(AccountFeed\|LegacyControlPlane)` in-process routing/bootstrap/permission checks after the identity changes. Not a full router suite, running server or actual PostgreSQL acceptance. |

Both the failed attempt and successful reruns remain as evidence. Compact output
does not enumerate per-test pass/skip counts, so none are inferred.

The same two source-only agents were reused and closed; the contribution count

## Claimed-Effect Prerequisite And Owner Isolation

This phase implements an exact-operation prerequisite, not the source-head fence
or product readiness. Current source and retained parent receipts supersede the
stale claim that final authorization lacks operation/version/claim binding.
The [source-only audit](claimed-effect-source-head-audit-20261001.md) remains
unchanged historical evidence; its line references describe its review snapshot.

### Implemented Source Contract

- Public managed background execution requires a live claim and a concrete broker
  before entering `WithClaimedSafeEffect`. The boundary verifies authoritative
  owner/workspace/operation/version, immutable identity/revision, running runtime/
  verification status and stored safe policy, then the exact live claim owner,
  generation and expiry. SQL locks operation before claim and retains its
  transaction across the synchronous broker callback; memory retains its lock.
  [Boundary source](../backend/internal/operations/claimed_effect_boundary.go),
  [managed dispatch](../backend/internal/background/execute.go).
- Private active context supplies the scope; caller JSON cannot create authority.
  The bridge discards inbound bindings, derives server scope and includes it in
  the managed contract-2 final-effect digest, including operation/version,
  owner/workspace, claim owner/generation and source identity/revision. It checks
  active scope at authorization/consumption, and the worker checks again before
  its first potentially mutating filesystem call. Scope expires on callback
  return or original authority cancellation/deadline, including detached contexts.
  [Digest](../backend/internal/executionbroker/authorization.go),
  [bridge](../backend/internal/executionbroker/executionauth_bridge.go),
  [worker](../backend/internal/executionbroker/local_safe_worker.go).
- Reserved `operation-` artifacts or `HAI-OP ` markers require managed scope even
  when an old version-1 receipt is already present. Unscoped ordinary ad-hoc
  artifacts retain contract 1; an active managed callback cannot downgrade to it.
  Scope/digest mutation and retained-context replay regressions are present in
  [operation scope tests](../backend/internal/executionbroker/operation_scope_test.go).
- Constitution evaluation now propagates the final-boundary context through
  executionauth, the adapter, frameworkregistry and GORM `WithContext` reads;
  repositories without contextual lookup fail closed, with no context-free
  fallback. The cooperative boundary budget is at most five seconds, further
  capped by caller deadline and conservative live lease expiry. This does not
  impose a hard deadline on synchronous filesystem syscalls.
  [Adapter](../backend/internal/executionauth/constitution_adapter.go),
  [contextual policy read](../backend/internal/frameworkregistry/service.go),
  [repository](../backend/internal/frameworkregistry/repository.go).
- Managed filenames now use the full operation UUID instead of an eight-character
  prefix; existing artifact overwrite/delete is not a reconciliation strategy.
  [Payload construction](../backend/internal/background/worker.go).
- All seven Runtime Lab HTTP read/mutation handlers compare the authenticated
  subject exactly with the configured single-operator owner before service access.
  Missing/malformed subjects receive 401, foreign subjects including privileged
  roles receive 403, and missing/malformed service owner configuration receives
  503. Router authentication/RBAC remain required; owner claims in headers/body/
  query cannot substitute for the verified context subject. Regression source
  covers refusal without adapter access, operation/audit mutation or attempt
  recording, plus exact-owner access. This gate does not cover direct in-process
  service/adapter calls. [Handlers](../backend/internal/runtimelab/handler.go),
  [owner regressions](../backend/internal/runtimelab/owner_gate_handler_test.go).

now require a non-nil, live context and acquire their real mutex through a
cancellable wait, without a detached goroutine. Writes recheck cancellation
before recording receipt, approval consumption or final exercise; canceled
mutators leave the existing maps unchanged. `Authorize`, `AuthorizeAndConsume`
and the final-effect bridge refuse absent/canceled contexts before accessing
policy/storage dependencies. This does not make every downstream dependency
preemptible or undo previously committed authority/effects.

### Audit Disposition And Remaining Limits

The historical Runtime Lab HTTP configured-owner finding, context-free final
Constitution dependency, detached-context authority extension and short artifact
UUID naming have source fixes above. The managed old-v1 nil-scope receipt bypass
was already corrected in the audit snapshot. This is not a declaration that all
findings or release gates are closed.

- **Source supersession remains open (gate 10).** Intake of revision B can create
  a separate row while A holds its own operation/claim locks. A remains eligible
  under the exact-operation prerequisite. Minimum next contract: unique tenant-
  scoped canonical source head, authoritative observation/config generation and
  replay ordering (including out-of-order fetch, A-B-A and tombstone cases), one
  consistent head-before-operation-before-claim lock order for all publishers/
  claim/effect paths, and head generation in the server-issued final-effect digest.
  Preserve old approvals/history and require renewed approval where applicable;
  explicitly classify source-less self-tests rather than guessing legacy identity.
- **Mode/block-rule final serialization remains open.** Upstream checks and final
  emergency-stop/Constitution rechecks do not serialize Phase 2 live mode and
  block-rule changes with the effect. Define controls for managed and ad-hoc paths;
  the HTTP owner gate does not close the direct in-process ad-hoc policy gap.
- **Pool capacity is not reserved.** The guard refuses only a positive configured
  maximum below two (an explicitly capped one-connection pool). Two or more, or
  unlimited, does not guarantee a spare connection for independent authorization
  while operation/claim locks are held. Saturated-pool contextual refusal is
  synthetic test coverage, not measured PostgreSQL admission or deadlock acceptance.
  Callbacks must remain synchronous and must not reenter locked repositories.
- **No exactly-once or crash-proof guarantee.** Cooperative cancellation can roll
  back SQL while an entered filesystem syscall continues; it cannot undo an effect
  or prove locks remained held through syscall completion. Independent durable
  authorization consumption and pre-dispatch intent must survive uncertain
  outcomes. Scope is not a one-shot attempt token; a fresh claim/version/receipt
  must not become replay permission. Durable semantic-effect/attempt consumption,
  uncertainty and explicit reconciliation invariants remain necessary.
- **Acceptance remains bounded.** Actual PostgreSQL boundary concurrency/crash and
  migration upgrade/rollback, real providers and signed clean-machine Windows
  install/deployment gates were not executed in this phase. Earlier session-store
  and synthetic recovery receipts do not accept this new boundary. No product-
  ready declaration, gate-10 closure, commit, push or deployment is implied.

SQL requires lowercase 64-hex configuration digests, positive generations and
nonzero observation/origin UUIDs. Clocks cannot change scope, reset, skip a
generation, be deleted or truncated; observations reject update/delete/truncate.
Operation observation ID/generation cannot change, including upgrading a legacy
NULL/zero row, and an observed operation's account-feed ID is frozen. The baseline SQL
formerly allowed an initially nil account-feed ID; current SQL closes that
origin gap. Identified observed operations require a nonnull, nonzero feed UUID,
and `(owner_user_id, workspace_id, source_observation_id,
source_observation_generation, account_feed_id)` references the observation's
unique `(owner_user_id, workspace_id, id, generation, origin_id)` tuple. A ticket
for another feed cannot bind this feed's operation, even in the same tenant.
Legacy NULL/zero observation groups remain valid without feed backfill. Both SQL
and application-minted observed sources require the matching origin. SQL checks do not recompute hashes or
claim complete parity with application UTF-8/control/byte-bound validation.

### Intake Authority Continuation

`intakeContextError` checks caller cancellation and wall-clock deadline and, when
the private observation key is present, requires its original server context to
remain active, uncanceled and unexpired. A stripped caller cancellation cannot
restore expired/ended original authority. `Service.IngestContext` does not turn a
nil context into `context.Background`; contextual memory/GORM lookup and audited
creation/update reject it before store access. Explicit legacy non-context APIs
retain their intentional Background entry path; no claim is made that every
internal API requires an observation.

Checks recur during memory lock waits and after acquisition, after service/GORM lookups and at
audited write boundaries; SQL transactional failures discard the operation/audit
pair. A lookup that returns normally after its absolute deadline must not return
an operation or proceed to create/refresh, including a read-only duplicate. Nil
GORM repository/database returns `ErrContextIntakeUnsupported` instead of panic
on these three contextual methods. This is cooperative authority validation,
not a hard deadline around a filesystem syscall or proof of crash safety.

`intake_authority_test.go` prepares 30 store refusal cases (two stores, five
authority states, three paths), five early service refusals, three late-lookup
service paths, nil GORM store cases and a separate delayed-expiry SQL-driver
lookup. Assertions require no operation/audit mutation and no SQL access for
early refusals. The later SQL-driver lookup checks that the original context is
used and expired results are discarded even while `ctx.Err()` remains nil.
The driver is synthetic: it is not real PostgreSQL acceptance. The latter test
was added after the first authority receipt and is covered by the subsequent
lock-reviewed receipt below. Two additional tests hold the actual memory mutex
while a detached context waits, end the original authority by server cancellation
or callback return, and require refusal before the mutex is released, with no
ledger mutation. This is real in-memory lock coverage, not SQL lock acceptance.

Hubble's finalized `backend/internal/operations/source_observation_origin_test.go`
is present and read: three top-level tests define 24 subcases (domain validation
three, memory creation three paths times five origins = fifteen, GORM creation
three paths times two invalid origins = six). Refused origins leave operations,
audits and observation clocks/tickets unchanged; exact-origin controls retain
healthy creation/binding/audit assertions. These local cases are now executed
successfully in the final eleven-package amendment suite. The nine embedded
observation migration contracts also execute in that suite; source counts are
not individual case listings in the compact log. The existing embedded contracts
and disposable PostgreSQL fixture now contain the tightened SQL origin cases;
actual SQL execution remains unaccepted. Final eleven-package validation now
passes after these amendments. Selected router success is separately recorded
below and does not execute these new operations tests.

## Execution Authority Continuity

This increment preserves authority across source observation, claim lifecycle,
claimed-effect callbacks, authorization receipts and local-worker execution.
This checkpoint preceded the accepted-source-head increment below.

- Missing, canceled, absolutely expired and ended private authority is rejected
  at service/repository claim entry, background-pass entry and execution entry.
  Missing contexts are not converted into fresh `context.Background()` authority.
- Binding retains caller values and both source-observation and claimed-effect
  scope. Original authority cancellation/deadlines remain effective even when a
  caller uses `context.WithoutCancel`; binding does not grant execution authority.
- Nested claimed-effect entry is refused before reacquiring operation/claim
  locks. Memory lock waits are cancellable; expired authority cannot publish
  staged memory recovery batches. SQL lifecycle mutations use transaction checks
  and bounded authority, retaining operation-before-claim lock order.
- Authorization issuance and consumption recheck authority around repository
  access. The local worker rechecks before dispatch, filesystem creation, write,
  sync and verification. Existing effect/consumption evidence is retained if
  authority ends after an irreversible step; failure does not mean no effect.

`operations/safe_effect_authority_test.go`,
`operations/execution_claim_authority_test.go`,
`operations/claim_service_authority_test.go`,
`executionbroker/execution_context_test.go` and
`background/execution_authority_test.go` (paths relative to `backend/internal`).
They include invalid/detached authority, held-lock cancellation, staged recovery,
synthetic SQL rollback, receipt-consumption uncertainty and a healthy nested
observation/claim/authorization/local-file/verification chain. Synthetic SQL is
not real PostgreSQL acceptance; temporary filesystem tests are not provider tests.

## Accepted Source Head Integration

This increment implements accepted-head supersession, not full production
acceptance or an exactly-once effect guarantee.

- Observation-backed intake requires head-publication capability. Older
  observations and stale configuration epochs are refused before operation
  creation or duplicate evidence refresh. Original provenance remains immutable.
- Scoped origins carry a monotonic configuration epoch; heads bind an exact
  observation, epoch, origin, revision and operation. Durable accepted-revision
  hashes detect semantic A-B-A recurrence. That recurrence persists a blocked
  reconciliation state instead of reviving an earlier executable operation.
- Claim preflight refuses missing, stale or blocked heads for identified work.
  The final effect rechecks the accepted head under origin/head/operation/claim
  locks, and the private managed-effect digest includes head generation and epoch.
  Preflight alone is not effect authorization.
- Migration 0113 is additive, preserves the existing five-field provenance FK,
  adds a six-field epoch-bound head-observation FK and immutable revision history.
  Historical observations remain epoch zero. Empty-only rollback refuses history;
  no migration was applied to a private database during this increment.
- Creation and acceptance use separate transactions. A partial identified
  operation can remain stored without an accepted head but cannot execute.

The reviewed receipt is
`production-low-resource-source-head-lifecycle-reviewed-final-20261001.receipt.json`.
Its log reports operations, accountfeed, background, migrations, executionbroker,
executionauth, frameworkregistry, phase2, opscontrol, runtimelab and automation
passing. Interval: 2026-10-01 20:00:38.6157051-20:01:32.3460534 UTC; exit 0;
no timeout. Native Go 1.25.13 used one worker, offline dependencies and a 384 MiB

## Canonical Registry Configuration Integration

This increment adds canonical configuration authority to the supported registry
repository path. It does not close gate 10 or establish production readiness.

- Migration `pre/0114_operation_source_configuration` adds positive feed
  configuration versions and managed-origin version/enablement. Feed changes,
  their audits and origin publication commit atomically in PostgreSQL with
  feed-row-before-origin locking. Memory composition holds both stores' locks
  through its non-reentrant commit callback. Final effects do not acquire the
  registry lock in reverse order.
- Canonical registration starts at version 1 and ignores supplied versions.
  Duplicate registration preserves stored configuration, version and audits.
  Normalized no-op patches preserve the version; actual multi-field changes
  increment once. Overflow, version regression and same-version configuration
  substitution are refused. A configuration A-B-A cycle cannot revive an old
  observation, even when the caller supplies zero/negative version or falsely
  claims the stored managed origin is unmanaged.
- Patch and disable revoke prior accepted-head authority immediately. Disabled
  sources refuse manual and bulk claims. An active import token remains owned;
  disabling does not clear another writer's claim. Managed-origin deletion,
  downgrade, identity change and unsafe rollback are refused. Existing history
  is neither backfilled nor deleted.
- Production router composition attaches the durable registry to both configured
  and owner-bound background workers. Tenant selection remains exact. Disabled
  feeds do not fall back to cached startup readers. A top-level registry failure
  stops the pass before existing queued work is processed, with a safe public
  error; synthetic tests assert unchanged stored tasks and audit history.
- Managed observation checks require the exact positive canonical version.
  Unexpected SQL lookup errors and cancellation are propagated, rather than
  reported as ordinary supersession. Populated unbound memory registries cannot
  silently enroll in a new ledger; read-only and same-ledger reconstruction are
  preserved, and different-ledger rebinding is refused.
- A canceled sync admission may already have committed a token. Fresh bounded
  cleanup settles only that exact owner/workspace/feed/token and appends its
  failed-attempt audit. It neither reads sources nor clears another attempt.
  Cleanup failure retains the token and reports operator review. `recorded`
  distinguishes stored outcome from `readCompleted` and successful intake.
- The private configuration digest uses a method-less Feed copy, avoiding the
  public `MarshalJSON` URL redaction. Secret URL changes therefore invalidate
  authority while public Feed/provenance JSON remains redacted. The regression
  deliberately compares identical public JSON with distinct private digests.

## Enrollment Serialization And Test-Database Safety

The preceding raw-enrollment source finding prompted a change to draft,
unapplied migration 0114. No private database or migration ledger was accessed.
The checker now serializes against the exact scoped feed before separately
reading the current managed origin. First enrollment performs a physical no-op
feed UPDATE, retaining its logical version. Other checks only lock/read, so an
origin enrollment queues a finite feed check rather than recursive feed writes.
Under repeatable read, a lock-only enrollment would not supply the row change
needed to invalidate an older writer's snapshot. This is the intended source
remedy, not an observed real-store result. PostgreSQL documents the relevant
[snapshot/write-conflict behavior](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/transaction-iso.html)
and [deferred versus immediate checks](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/sql-set-constraints.html).

Guarded acceptance definitions now cover 50 named leaf cases: 24 coordinated
enrollment/mutation schedules, two stale-repeatable-read TRUNCATE refusals, and
24 enrollment-first cases for default deferred COMMIT and already-immediate
origin DML. The latter exercise healthy enrollment plus exact refusal of a
stale rename, disable or delete; they do not cover mutation-first firing-mode
schedules. Independent owned connections, PID-bound lock observation,
statement/context deadlines, worker joins and exact committed/rolled-back
state checks are explicit. Source inspection and skipped SQL cases do not
establish runtime concurrency, progress, or crash safety.

The shared migration test helper no longer changes its administration database
to `postgres`, accepts hostname destinations, or forcibly drops a database.
It requires the central explicit destructive-test guard and exact literal
loopback `hai_migration_runner_test` identity before opening either pool.
It creates a UUID-named database from `template0`, records its OID, verifies
the connected identity/namespace, caps administration/test pools at 1/4, and
uses bounded deadlines. Cleanup closes test pools first and rechecks owned OID
under a fresh 15-second context; missing/mismatched ownership refuses cleanup.
No FORCE, CASCADE, session termination or pre-existing database reuse is allowed.
These ownership checks assume a cooperative isolated operator environment;
they are not an atomic defense against a privileged simultaneous database
rename/replacement. Diagnostics expose SQLSTATE/deadline classes, not private
connection details.

CI now creates a separate `hai_account_feed_registry_test` administration
database, runs actual registry reconstruction/atomic-failure acceptance, and
requires explicit PASS markers for each configuration test and all 50 leaf
cases. This configuration has not been executed on GitHub in this increment.
SQL opt-in remains disabled locally. No source-only assertion, overall green
unit result, or optional test skip is counted as PostgreSQL acceptance.

## Live Final-Effect Control Serialization

The next source audit found two in-process races. A mode restriction could
finish after a worker's policy read but before its filesystem effect. A request
classified as restrictive against an old mode could overwrite a newer stricter
mode without escalation authorization. Failed mode persistence could also
leave the old cached permission usable despite untrusted persisted state.

Scheduled workers, manual operation runs and Runtime Lab self-tests now provide
exactly one live server policy to `ExecuteSafeOperationClaimed`. Real broker
execution without that policy fails before operation transitions. After the
repository locks the operation/claim and validates current source authority,
execution acquires the existing context-bounded shared commit fence, rechecks
mode/stop/block policy and retains the fence through bounded file work and
broker verification. Seed/set/CAS mode writers and block-rule additions use its
exclusive side before acquiring their own mutexes. Stop persistence already used
that exclusive side. Authorization callbacks run outside controller mutation
locks; the effect path acquires the shared fence once, avoiding recursive reader
acquisition when a writer is queued. Policies must read only, not mutate controls
or reenter the locked operation repository.

All service mode changes, including restrictions and persistence repairs, now
compare-and-swap the original decision snapshot. Failed writes retain the old
raw cached value only as historical state: effective/stored-status reads return
paused plus a persistence error until repaired. A valid version number cannot
substitute different title, description or operation type at the safe-effect
boundary, since these are inputs to live risk/block policy.

New local regressions cover missing/nil/ambiguous final policy, restriction-first
refusal before authorization/filesystem effect, effect-first fence retention
through real temporary-file work, block-rule and mode mutation ordering, stale
restriction/repair refusal, authorization callback interleaving, persistence
failure for both setters, and substituted policy content for legacy/identified
operations. A source AST contract ties all three service mutation paths to CAS;
it is not an executed concurrent service restriction test. The existing failure
test was corrected to require fail-closed paused state rather than treating
retained cached permission as the intended result.

This compiles the composed router; the synthetic route tree is not a live

was performed. Joyce and ShareT were not stopped.

This ordering applies to the shared in-process managed operation path, not to
independent controllers/processes, arbitrary standalone broker users, other
runtime adapters or external filesystem state. Mode/stop writers wait for an
already-admitted effect; this is not immediate preemption of an entered syscall.
Exclusive writer waits remain non-cancellable. Context/lease checks bound
admission and subsequent observations but cannot cancel a blocking OS call.
Real PostgreSQL crash/concurrency, cross-process control/identity reconciliation,
durable effect replay, spare pool capacity and irreversible-effect uncertainty
remain release gates. No full production-ready claim is made.

## Backend PostgreSQL Resource Bounds

The source audit found uncapped backend pools, an automatic Gorm ping outside
caller cancellation, failed initialization ownership and uncached migration
failure leaks. Constructor arguments were interpolated into a keyword DSN;
passwords containing spaces or connection-option text could alter parsing.

`NewPostgresDatabaseContext` now owns the SQL pool until successful return. It
validates settings, encodes connection fields separately, configures limits
before first use, disables Gorm's automatic ping and explicitly pings within the
earlier caller/open deadline. A connector wrapper bounds the entire physical
connection acquisition, including DNS and pgx multi-host fallback; the driver's
own timeout alone applies per host after resolution. Initialization failure
closes its owned pool, and `GetDefaultDB` closes uncached pools after migration
failure while preserving the original error. Successful default pools remain
cached for process lifetime; repositories do not close borrowed shared handles.
The health probe passes its caller context through constructor entry.

Default settings, forwarded by the canonical Compose API and migration job:

| Backend setting | Default | Validation |
|---|---|---|
| `DB_MAX_OPEN_CONNS` | `8` | Integer at least 2; no unlimited 0 |
| `DB_MAX_IDLE_CONNS` | `2` | Integer 0 through max open |
| `DB_CONN_MAX_IDLE_TIME` | `2m` | Positive duration with units |
| `DB_CONN_MAX_LIFETIME` | `30m` | Positive duration with units |
| `DB_CONNECT_TIMEOUT` | `5s` | Positive duration with units |
| `DB_OPEN_TIMEOUT` | `10s` | Positive duration with units |

These are conservative defaults, not measured production sizing. Empty settings
use defaults; malformed values do not silently restore unlimited pools. Errors
identify the setting key, not its raw input. Structured-URI parse failures do not
include the credential-bearing URI in the returned error.

Native regressions use a network-free driver with real `database/sql` pooling:
capacity exhaustion observes caller cancellation; excess idle connections are
closed; idle/total lifetimes expire; ping/connection failures and deadlines close
owned connections; migration retries close only the failed pool and retain the
successful cached one. Separate pgx parsing tests preserve special credentials,
IPv6/socket hosts and database names without injecting timezone/TLS options.
The health cancellation regression deliberately rejects before DNS/connection.
A Python Compose contract checks every setting in API/migration services and
does not misrepresent the separate IDP as covered by backend limits.

added before rerunning the final suite. The agent was then closed. No new or
nested agents were spawned. These are local network-free/synthetic regressions,
not PostgreSQL acceptance. Final verification evidence is linked below.
No real PostgreSQL connection, migration, Docker startup/download, deployment,
commit or push was performed. Joyce and ShareT remained untouched, HAI stopped.

max capacity >= 2 does not reserve a connection for independent authorization:
simultaneous effect transactions can exhaust the pool. All authorization-policy
repositories must share a safe reserved-connection/admission strategy before
that progress gate can be accepted; no effect transaction may absorb the
independent durable authorization commit. Database lock cycles, crash/restore
acceptance and measured resource savings also remain open.

## Reserved Authorization Connection Admission

The pool-cap baseline above did not reserve independent authorization capacity.
The subsequent source change adds `infra.WithPostgresExecutionConnections` to
the managed Gorm claimed-effect boundary. It checks out both connections before
row locks, serializing only pair acquisition per pool to prevent two workers
from each holding the first connection while awaiting the second. Waiting is
caller-bounded; a partial checkout is released without entering the callback.
The gate and both borrowed connections are released on return. Successful cleanup
preserves the original callback error identity.

The lock transaction retains its own connection. Receipt persistence/consumption,
task-review authorization, final-effect exercise, constitution/selection reads,
framework-evidence resolution and source-evidence resolution bind to the reserved
authorization connection. Authorization transactions keep their own commit and
original pool identity; a lock transaction, mismatched pool, expired reservation
or private handle without its authority context is rejected rather than redirected.
Shared Gorm handles are not mutated. The explicit caller-transaction path binds
authority before resolving mutable task review, not after that first query.

The private deadline can only shorten. A reservation-owned timer cancels already
issued bound query contexts at the tightened deadline, including contexts detached
from caller cancellation. Future bindings also refuse expired authority. Callbacks
remain synchronous and must not retain rows, handles, transactions or asynchronous
work. This is cooperative database cancellation, not OS syscall preemption.

Two existing source-only agents reviewed disjoint wiring and were closed after
their final reviews. The parent fixed pre-validation task-review access, cancellation
of already-bound handles and a weak concurrency fixture. The revised test pauses
the first worker during its second physical checkout, then checks that a competing
worker waits at pair admission without entering the SQL pool or acquiring locks.
Its failure paths cancel and join owned workers.

and [results](production-low-resource-reserved-connections-reviewed-20261001.log).

durability, approval, deployment or real-provider acceptance. An additional
private-handle rejection regression was added after this reviewed pass; final
integrated evidence follows. Advisory post-commit projection retains the caller's
default context instead of inheriting the temporary query binding cancelled at
transactional authorization return.

- All 85 Python CI-contract tests passed locally. These assert source/deployment

Open boundaries: reservations are in-process and per pool; unrelated pool users
may delay admission, and multiple processes still need actual SQL concurrency
acceptance. Arbitrary external/custom resolvers are not made safe by this wiring.
Pool shutdown, cross-process control, restore/crash behavior, irreversible effect
uncertainty and measured resource savings remain open. No Docker startup, private

remain stopped; Joyce and ShareT remain running and untouched.

## Managed SQL Authority And Collaborator Affinity

The next source audit found remaining bypasses behind the previously safe normal
repository path. Anonymous SQL embeddings promoted unchecked query and transaction
methods. A caller could replace a Gorm context, begin a detached transaction or
extract the unrestricted pool. Agent-rights, mandate decisions and graph-enabled
projection could also demand a third connection despite two reserved connections.

Scoped wrappers now use private named SQL fields and explicitly implement Gorm's
required entry points. SQL and transaction begin require the active private state
and the cancellation channel originally issued by the reservation or binder.
Replaced/detached channels are rejected before driver entry; descendants can be
legitimately rebound through the repository helper, which restores cancellation
and the bounded deadline. Commit checks the issued begin authority and refuses
expired work; rollback remains available after revocation. Cancellation before
connection closure allows `database/sql` to clean unfinished transactions/rows.

`GetDBConn` refuses scoped pool extraction. The existing executionauth/task
genuine-transaction guards remain and use an opaque pool-identity comparison.
Pool identity is not authority: another reservation on the same pool still fails
context/transaction binding. No unrestricted pool is returned by that comparison.

Fresh private Gorm configurations/caches prevent a reserved prepared session from
borrowing a previously cached unrestricted statement. Prepared statements are
unsupported in this boundary. A private Row callback avoids Gorm v1.25.10's empty
`sql.Row` preparation-failure path, returning a scannable error without executing
SQL. Ordinary prepared sessions outside reservations remain operational.
Canonical PostgreSQL dialect options and exported configuration are retained;
registered custom Gorm plugins are refused, not silently reinitialized. Custom
callbacks/plugins and arbitrary separately held SQL pools are not made safe by
this path. Fresh schema caches have not been benchmarked for production cost.

Agent `Get`/`GetAssignment`, mandate `Get`/`CreateDecision`/`GetDecision`, and six
projection-used lifeontology methods now bind their SQL handles, including
duplicate fallback reads. Explicit transactional authorization installs private
transaction affinity before evaluating collaborators. Their reads/writes then
reuse the independently owned authorization transaction, not its bare connection
or the locked effect transaction. Post-commit advisory projection uses the outer
context after the authorization commit. This supports optional graph/agent/mandate
compositions; it does not claim the default Phase 2 composition enables them all.

Two existing source-only agents performed independent reviews; one implemented
only the lifeontology repository binding. Both were closed after parent review.
A mistaken callback-isolation finding was checked against the actual dependency
source and retracted: Gorm stores the callback registry on the Config copied by
the binder. No production code was changed on the basis of that mistaken finding.

## HTTP Shutdown Ownership

The October 2 local resource audit found that `Serve` returned as soon as
`Shutdown` closed its listener, before active requests drained. The API now joins
the shutdown result on cancellation and on unexpected listener failure, preserves
the original listener error, and reports drain failure while closing remaining
ordinary HTTP sockets. The default drain allowance remains 20 seconds. The
initialization child context is also cancelled on every exit, including partially
initialized startup; cancellation alone does not join scheduler goroutines.

Real loopback HTTP regression cases hold a live request while the listener closes,
then verify successful drain, timeout-induced connection closure with a deadline
error, and permanent listener failure with preservation of the original error.
Additional cases cover idle cancellation, a closed listener, invalid arguments,
configured server timeouts and platform shutdown signals. This is local HTTP
behavior, not Docker, database, provider, browser or Windows installer acceptance.

The first native focused run passed (exit 0, no timeout), recorded by
`production-low-resource-http-shutdown-ownership-20261002-first-20261001.receipt.json`,
22:11:29.843-22:13:18.901 UTC October 1 (October 2 locally). Two source-only reused
reviewers identified the listener-failure path and test-cleanup/status gaps; these
were incorporated. Final reviewed-source verification is recorded separately below.

The reviewed native run also passed (exit 0, no timeout), recorded by
`production-low-resource-http-shutdown-ownership-20261002-reviewed-20261001.receipt.json`,
22:13:38.644-22:14:21.823 UTC October 1. Both runs selected only the HTTP lifecycle,
server timeout and shutdown signal tests in the router package. They used one Go
worker, offline dependencies, disabled destructive database tests and a 384 MiB
soft heap limit; no whole-product regression or measured savings claim is made.
Formatting and scoped tracked whitespace checks were clean. Both reused reviewers
were closed, and no owned portable-SDK or router-test process remained. The final
Docker snapshot showed only Joyce's four and ShareT's three containers running,
with all ten HAI containers stopped. ShareT was health-starting; this audit did

verification container observed earlier was not stopped or removed by this turn.

The allowance bounds graceful request draining, not every possible custom listener
operation or handler lifetime. Hijacked connections, uncooperative handlers,
background-worker joins and shared PostgreSQL pool shutdown remain separate gates.
No service was started or redeployed, no database/volume was removed, and no commit

## One-Shot Command Safety And Pool Ownership

The October 2 local audit confirmed a safety mismatch: `reconcile` was documented
as a dry-run but used the cached startup opener, which can run migrations.
Reconciliation now uses its own context-aware, migration-free pool and requests
a read-only transaction for the scan. Startup cached-pool behavior is unchanged.
Command contexts are cancelled and owned pools closed before the enclosing `os.Exit`,
including read, apply, rollback and status failure paths; close errors turn success
into a nonzero exit. `migrate up` now renders status on that same pool, not through
a recursive invocation that opens another one.

Top-level command and migration argument validation precedes configuration.
Unknown commands no longer start the server. Unsupported extra arguments and flags
(including `migrate up --dry-run` and `down <target> --dry-run`) are refused, not
silently treated as permission to mutate. `HAI_COMMAND_TIMEOUT` defaults to a
positive 30-minute duration and is propagated through acquisition, queries and
migration transactions. The example configuration and Compose API/migration job
forward it. These are direct privileged administrator commands, not dashboard
approval-gated actions; actual mutation still requires deliberate authorization.

configuration poison; both were corrected. Tests now use fresh bounded closure

Migration apply/rollback callbacks in these tests are deliberately simulated;

crash recovery or commit behavior. The real opener's already-cancelled/nil-context
checks exercise refusal before any database connection.

## Streaming Memory Reconciliation

The October 2 continuation replaces the full `FindAll()` allocation in the CLI with
a single read-only SQL row stream. Only ID, content, kind, confidence and tags are
selected. The existing all-record scope and `updated_at desc` ordering remain:
there is no limit, owner filter or archived-record exclusion. Each fresh row passes
through the same pure `ScanMemory` rules used by batch `ScanMemories`. The batch API
still accumulates its report; only the CLI now retains counters rather than the
corpus and all findings.

The CLI prints a provisional header with the first finding and emits each finding
before proceeding. Only successful iteration, row closure and transaction commit
permit final counts. Late read/conversion/close/commit failures, short writes,
output failures and cancellation return failure instead of an unqualified clean
report. Context is checked before clean output and on return, including when a
writer cancels it during output. Already-written bytes cannot be withdrawn, so
the process exit status remains authoritative.

The audit also found two confidence-validation defects. NaN was previously accepted
because it compares neither below zero nor above one; it and both infinities now
receive a `finite` violation requiring verified-source manual review. The existing
schema permits SQL NULL confidence. A private nullable scan row preserves this
distinction from valid zero, stops the report as incomplete, and requests a source-
based correction; no public model/API schema, database record or migration changes.
The tag repair proposal now correctly says 512 bytes, not characters, and avoids
proposing a split UTF-8 character. No proposed repair is applied by reconciliation.

Synthetic fixtures exercise actual Gorm/database/sql code without a server. A lazy
5,000-record generator produces fresh approximately 16 KiB content per row; its
writer asserts each finding arrives before the next generated row, retains no
output list, and checks that source content is not emitted. Commit/rollback hooks
check row closure at the finalization boundary. Fixtures reject incomplete row
shapes rather than inheriting database/sql destination values. Separate cases cover
nullable fields, explicit zero followed by NULL confidence, late read errors,
conversion errors, close/commit errors, partial writes, output cancellation, empty
corpus and non-finite confidence. This proves the local streaming/error paths, not
real PostgreSQL read-only enforcement, driver timing or measured peak memory.

identified and confirmed corrections; source review itself is not runtime proof.
CI/source contracts separately passed all 86 tests in 2.219 seconds.

## Registered Runtime Drain And Terminal Pool Shutdown

This extends the earlier HTTP-only shutdown work. The API composition owns a
`lifecycle.Group`, seals admission on shutdown, cancels scheduler contexts, and
joins registered work before invoking the shared database and Redis rate-limiter
closers. The middleware tracks actual handler return, independently of HTTP
socket draining. Detached children retain ownership through `WithoutCancel`;
ownership attachment does not replace an existing request's cancellation or
another caller's owning group. Drain timeout reports the outstanding internal
labels and skips pool closure. HTTP and runtime drain each allow 20 seconds;
the backend Compose service now allows a 60-second stop grace.

Tracked paths include source, workflow, ambient and ambient-monitor workers;
host/OpenClaw reconciliation; catalog, model and OpenClaw maintenance; provider
probes; durable handlers that return after their polling loop; OAuth refreshes;
readiness callbacks; Temporal supervision and activities; emergency-stop polling;
and installer callbacks that have not confirmed cancellation. Installer ownership
does not convert an uncertain external outcome into success or proof of process
termination. Background claim-heartbeat cleanup cancels and joins on a processing
panic. Existing completion/renewal classification is preserved: a late renewal
after a successful claim release is not newly reported as a processing failure.

Source services bind their compatibility owner during API composition. Legacy
sync and scheduling retain that runtime's cancellation; context-aware scheduling
preserves request values and owner scoping. Expired caller deadlines are checked
before admission and remain `DeadlineExceeded`. Runtime-stop refusal aborts a
sweep without inventing a provider failure or creating failure-workflow writes.
A source-local deadline with a still-live caller remains an operational failure,
so one timed-out source does not conceal its failure or incorrectly cancel the
entire sweep. Permission monitors check cancellation again before starting work
after a potentially blocking control read.

The shared backend pool now initializes single-flight and closes outside its
cache mutex. Shutdown is terminal for that process: pending candidates cannot be
published after sealing, failed candidates are closed, repeated closers retain
the close outcome, and a `(nil, nil)` opener is rejected before migrations.
Synthetic tests use actual `database/sql` pooling without a PostgreSQL server.
At this stage startup migrations were context-free. The subsequent cancellation
follow-up below replaces that API path. Uncooperative SQL callbacks and driver
close still can delay cleanup; there is no universal termination guarantee.

## Cancellable Startup And Frontend Release Controls

The shared pool now exposes `GetDefaultDBContext`. API composition acquires the
pool with its signal-owned context before constructing context-free default
repositories. Pure metrics configuration validation happens before database
acquisition, so an invalid Prometheus token setting does not first run migrations.
The first initializer forwards caller context through opening and migrations,
with a whole-startup `DB_STARTUP_TIMEOUT` default of five minutes. Invalid,
zero or overflowing durations fail before opening and do not echo raw values.
Earlier caller cancellation/deadlines win. A waiting caller can cancel its own
wait without cancelling the initializer. Terminal close cancels pending startup
and still joins actual cleanup; callbacks that ignore cancellation are not
pretended to be terminated. Failed/expired candidates close before a retry can
begin; a successful hook cannot override expired authority and publish a pool.

`RunMigrationsContext` binds a Gorm clone, retaining a neutral cached runtime
handle after the startup caller returns/cancels. Default Compose still disables
API migrations and uses its separate one-shot command deadline. Each versioned
migration remains separately transactional: canceling initialization does not
undo earlier migrations that already committed. Real PostgreSQL advisory-lock
cancellation, transactional rollback and deployment remain acceptance gates.

Source-only frontend review found unversioned assets incorrectly treated as
year-long immutable files, including favicon; `always` also attached immutable
headers to errors. Nginx now limits that policy to content-hashed root bundle
names, revalidates other static assets, and omits immutable headers on errors.
HTML no-store and SPA deep-link fallback remain unchanged. Cache-policy checks
exercise the source patterns and precedence, not a live Nginx process.

Native build/watch scripts now launch the local Angular CLI through a small
validated worker guard, defaulting to one worker. Existing Docker build choice
defaults to two. Explicit choices `1`, `2`, `4`, `8` are accepted; conflicting
or invalid HAI/Angular overrides fail before launch. Environment and arguments
are preserved without shell expansion; launch failures, compiler status and
interruptions cannot silently become success. This bounds worker count, not
absolute CPU/RAM, and does not establish the requested 90% reduction. Direct
operator CLI invocations and unrelated applications are outside this guard.

## Control-Room Routing And Truth Review Access

Local source round: 2026-10-02 Europe/Amsterdam (checked through
2026-10-01 23:43 UTC). The original five class/pipeline regressions failed on
the current source before repair and all passed after the production changes.

- Module matching now uses Angular's `DefaultUrlSerializer` and primary route
  segment paths. Matrix parameters no longer borrow Command Center preferences;
  encoded slashes remain inside their original segment and malformed URLs fail
  to the existing fallback.
- Truth review clears old session authority before refreshing and cancels a
  superseded queue subscription. Approval requires authenticated identity,
  nonempty subject, verified permission, no pending refresh and no authority error.
- Correction and lifecycle 401/403 responses revoke stale approval authority.
  Correction rejection preserves the editable draft and clears its confirmation.
  Page/drawer recovery actions allow an explicit refresh or sign-in, never an
  automatic correction retry. A fresh successful read does not reconfirm the draft.
- Detail requests are cancelled when another record opens, the inspector closes,
  or the component is destroyed. Invalid effective dates are rejected before
  the request starts, instead of throwing after setting the saving state.
- Native build/watch execution and the production prebuild reject Node versions
  outside the real package engine policy using the installed CLI's existing
  semver dependency. The host Node 25.2.1 was rejected without starting Angular;
  the already bundled Node 24.19.0 was used for the lightweight tests.

## Deferred Deep-Link Focus Ownership

Source round: 2026-10-02 Europe/Amsterdam, checked through
2026-10-01 23:50 UTC. This closes the implementation defect listed in the previous
section, not its rendered acceptance gate.

- AppShell now owns the pending deep-link restoration. It observes arriving
  sections and disclosure state for at most 30 seconds, coalescing mutations into
  one scheduled attempt. It waits for the actual summary/trigger rather than
  focusing an empty progressive-component host.
- Focus is attempted only for connected, rendered, enabled, non-inert targets
  while the document is visible. A successful focus is followed by scroll and
  complete observer/timer/listener cleanup; later record updates cannot refocus it.
- Trusted pointer, key and wheel events cancel pending restoration. Programmatic
  disclosure clicks do not cancel their own restoration. Route change, module
  reset, cleared fragment and component destruction also invalidate it.
- The independent delayed scroll in progressive sections was removed. This
  prevents late components from bypassing shell cancellation. Ordinary module
  navigation also checks its generation before its scheduled main-content focus.
- Node component-logic loading is shared by the two lightweight regression files
  and still imports real Angular/RxJS dependencies. Its DOM, events and timers are
  fixtures, not a real browser. Initial test-harness issues (incomplete outlet
  fixture and compiler import order) were corrected; these were not counted as
  product defects. Missing-target regressions then failed on the old logic and
  passed after the production change.
- Browser suite inventory readers now explicitly load Angular's compiler before
  the registry's real router parser; both existing suites load successfully with
  the compatible bundled Node runtime.
