## Credential Redaction Across Runtime And Registry Inspection

Source round: October 2, 2026, Europe/Amsterdam. The preceding goal turn changed
focus recovery and added real strict compiler checks; it was progress, not an
impasse. This round audits the shared backend secret filter and the separate
Framework Registry inbound presentation boundary without starting the app.

The backend redactor did not recognize structured Cookie/Set-Cookie fields,
multi-credential cookie lines, fine-grained GitHub token prefixes, or private-key
blocks truncated before their END marker. New baseline tests reproduced the
cookie and incomplete-key defects before repair (terminal failure retained in
`production-low-resource-secret-boundary-baseline-oct02-20261001.*`).

Changes integrated into the current source:

- `safety.RedactSecrets` removes complete cookie header/assignment lines instead
  of stopping at the first semicolon. Cookie field aliases include `cookie`,
  `set-cookie`, `set_cookie` and `setCookie`; the JSON path and URL query policy
  use the existing sensitive-key check.
- Header matching requires a line/whitespace boundary so a cookie URL query
  does not consume unrelated query parameters. The URL regression preserves
  the ordinary `project` parameter.
- Fine-grained `github_pat_` tokens join the existing provider-token patterns.
- Incomplete PEM private-key blocks discard the remainder of the untrusted
  text; complete blocks preserve the following diagnostic context. Public
  certificates remain unchanged. Size/depth bounds and no-op JSON compatibility
  remain in the existing filter.
- Framework Registry inbound sanitization separately removes Set-Cookie
  fields, all cookie header values, and complete/incomplete private-key blocks.
  Synthetic reads through the actual public service method preserve enabled,
  authority and other operational contract fields without mutating the source
  object. Three new actual-service tests failed before this repair and pass now.
- The new lightweight service cases run in both the frontend test chain and CI.
  The read-only strict compiler configs now include the Framework Registry
  service and its existing Jasmine spec: four production roots and four spec
  roots. Full build and rendered tests remain mandatory separate CI gates.

Fresh verification:

- Final native Windows Go receipt:
  `production-low-resource-secret-boundary-final-oct02-20261001.receipt.json`.
  All five selected package suites passed: safety, frameworkregistry,
  agentruntime, task and router. Terminal exit 0, no timeout, one Go worker,
  offline dependencies, destructive database tests disabled, no integration
  build tag. This does not prove PostgreSQL/live-provider acceptance. The

- Real Angular template/TypeScript check passed with four production roots;
  strict TypeScript check passed with four selected Jasmine spec roots. Both

  credentials hidden in escaped/nested names cannot survive unchanged-key

  Ordinary metadata, unchanged JSON bytes, size/depth guards and repeated
  filtering remain covered by the existing compatibility regressions.

  assignments, nested JSON field names and directly populated detail maps.

## Temporal Schedule Truth And Conditional Settlement

Continuing the production goal on 2026-10-02 with no new agents, Docker starts,
downloads or private/live database access. The previous cleanup turn refreshed
the host inventory: only Joyce's four and ShareT's three protected containers
were running; HAI was stopped. This turn made local source/test changes only.

Reproduced failures, retained in
`production-low-resource-temporal-schedule-truth-baseline-oct02-20261001.*`:

- Denied authorization plus a failed rejection write left a falsely `scheduled`
  row. Initial storage also claimed `scheduled` during approval and before SDK
  acceptance.
- A simulated timeout after dispatch was treated as a definitive rejection,
  even though the accepted effect was not reconciled.
- A worker finishing during the SDK call left a completed database row but the
  HTTP service returned its stale scheduled snapshot.
- The real activity method accepted preparing/unknown/unstarted-failed rows,
  and its completed shortcut accepted a record with missing owner provenance.

Implemented and locally verified:

- New schedules start `preparing`, pass the exact-effect authorizer, and confirm
  a durable `dispatching` fence before the emergency-stop check and SDK call.
  Authorization/stop rejection cleanup is checked and failures surface safely.
- Scheduling-only mutations use one conditional GORM update, fenced by run ID,
  owner, workflow ID/type, scheduled time, previous state and absent worker
  timestamps. Only status, summary and updated-at are changed. No whole-row Save
  settles a late SDK response over worker progress.
- A fresh context-aware, owner-scoped read confirms transition provenance. Nil,
  wrong-identity/time/type and conflicting replies cannot reach dispatch.
- Confirmed acceptance settles to `scheduled` or returns actual concurrent
  running/completed/failed activity progress. Uncertain SDK replies settle to
  `schedule_uncertain`; failed settlement can remain `dispatching`.
- Attempted-effect uncertainty returns safe HTTP 503, stable run/workflow IDs
  and `retrySafe: false`. Provider/database error details are not exposed, and
  no second scheduling call is made. Client cancellation does not cancel the
  fresh three-second bounded settlement context.
- Already-canceled requests do not create or authorize work. The activity
  rejects nil/wrong-ID/owner/type and non-dispatch states before proposal
  generation; completed replay validates owner/type, timestamps and JSON.
- UTC microsecond normalization is identical across storage, effect binding and
  SDK input. A round trip through the installed pgx binary timestamptz codec
  independently checks the driver precision rule without a database connection.
- New actual-handler/service regressions cover failed cleanup, pre-dispatch
  write failures/conflicts, post-acceptance failures, canceled transports, seven
  read-provenance mutations and all three concurrent worker outcomes. Actual
  GORM dry-run SQL assertions cover predicate/column scope, result mapping and
  forbidden transitions, not real PostgreSQL locking.
- README and `docs/temporal-durability.md` explain the states, uncertainty,
  precision and legacy limits instead of claiming retry safety or exactly-once.

Verification:

- `production-low-resource-temporal-schedule-truth-replay-final-oct02-20261001.receipt.json`:
  temporalbridge, apierror, safety and router suites passed; terminal exit 0,
  no timeout, one native Windows worker, offline dependencies, 384 MiB soft heap
  limit, no integration build tag or destructive database tests. Baseline and
  intermediate receipts retained. Eight malformed completed-record cases are
  refused before success/re-execution, and valid completed replay returns the
  recorded result without another proposal-generation call.
- `python scripts/test_ci_contract.py`: 88 tests passed, terminal exit 0.
- Focused whitespace checks passed; ordinary Windows line-ending warnings are
  not test failures. No browser, frontend rebuild, deployment, commit or push.

Remaining acceptance is not reduced: injected repositories/schedulers and SDK
virtual tests are not live Temporal/PostgreSQL evidence. Real crash-after-start
and read/write ambiguity, provider/database reconciliation, legacy scheduled
rows' approval/dispatch provenance, durable activity authorization and execution
claim fencing remain unaccepted. Historic rows were not automatically relabeled
or reauthorized; a legacy `scheduled` row alone is not proof under the new
contract. The activity still uses its existing worker persistence path, separate
from this scheduling fence. Cross-request scheduling deduplication and
exactly-once proposal generation are not claimed. All gates below stay open.

## Temporal Worker Startup Ownership And Honest Pending State

Continued on 2026-10-02 under the low-resource restriction: no new agents,
Docker starts/builds/downloads, private database access, migrations, deployment,
restart, commit or push. HAI remains stopped; Joyce and ShareT remain untouched.

Reproduced before fixes:

- `production-low-resource-temporal-startup-boundary-baseline-oct02-20261001.receipt.json`:
  terminal exit 1 without verifier timeout. Held synthetic dial exposed blocked
  status reads, absent dial deadline, shutdown unable to cancel pending dial,
  missing manual-start lifecycle ownership and a queued duplicate dial.
- `production-low-resource-temporal-startup-dependencies-baseline-oct02-20261001.receipt.json`:
  terminal exit 1. Missing repository/workflow wiring still reached the fake SDK
  and reported a started worker. These are injected boundary tests, not a live
  Temporal server or PostgreSQL acceptance run.

Implemented:

- SDK work and cleanup no longer hold the status mutex. Concurrent startup
  requests share the pending attempt rather than queue another dial.
- Dial and health use a ten-second attempt budget; health has a three-second
  maximum within that budget. Earlier request deadlines and host shutdown cancel
  the attempt. Missing governed dependencies are rejected before SDK calls.
- Manual startup enters the host lifecycle join. Pending ownership remains until
  SDK cleanup returns, including partially started and late-returning workers.
  Shutdown seals restart; a late SDK success cannot publish a stopped worker.
- Published activity lifetime belongs to the host, not the HTTP request/probe
  deadline. Request cancellation still prevents an unaccepted worker from being
  published. Nil/partial SDK results and failed startup receive owned cleanup.
- `workerStarting` describes pending startup/cleanup, not readiness. The actual
  handler returns HTTP 202 for pending startup, 503 for known failure/shutdown,
  and 200 for a successfully published worker. Known errors remain visible while
  cleanup is held; retry cannot overlap that cleanup.
- README and `docs/temporal-durability.md` document the contract and limitations.

Verification:

- `production-low-resource-temporal-startup-boundary-final-oct02-20261001.receipt.json`:
  temporalbridge, lifecycle, router and safety suites passed; terminal exit 0,
  no timeout, one native Windows Go worker, offline dependencies, 384 MiB soft
  heap limit, no integration build tag or destructive database tests.
- CI contract check initially passed 87 tests and failed one stale assertion
  requiring the old context variable. The assertion now verifies host ownership,
  rejects activity ownership by the probe context and checks lifecycle admission.
  The subsequent run passed all 88 tests, terminal exit 0.
- Existing CI race coverage now includes lifecycle and temporalbridge. This is
  configured coverage only: neither the remote CI race job nor a local race
  detector run was executed in this pass.

Installed Temporal SDK v1.41.1 source uses contextless/background-context work
inside worker Start/Stop. Therefore the ten-second budget bounds dial/health I/O,
not the entire SDK start/shutdown path. No detached timeout goroutine is used to
claim false completion: unfinished SDK cleanup remains owned and an incomplete
host drain remains reportable. Real SDK/server timing, PostgreSQL crash recovery,
durable activity authorization/claims and legacy approval provenance remain open.
No frontend rebuild, browser acceptance or live provider acceptance is claimed.

Final read-only process snapshot: no HAI container or native Go verifier process
was running. Joyce and ShareT containers were running; ShareT app health was
starting, not independently accepted. A separate Cofounder test database was
also running and was left untouched because ownership/use was not established.
No volume, source file or diagnostic artifact was removed during this pass.

## Temporal Activity Conditional Claim And Settlement

Continued on 2026-10-02 with no new agents, Docker starts/builds/downloads,
private-store tests/migrations, deployment, commit or push. The preceding turn
made source/test progress by repairing the actual workflow input link and null
completion replay, without claiming production acceptance. This pass addresses
the remaining activity write/retry boundary rather than restating that result.

Reproduced before production edits:

- `production-low-resource-temporal-activity-claim-baseline-oct02-20261001.receipt.json`:
  terminal exit 1 without timeout. Cancellation triggered during record lookup
  still caused one proposal-service call and two whole-row writes.
- A second activity invocation while the first service call was held caused two
  proposal-service calls. An interrupted `running` row was also blindly executed
  again. These are actual activity-method paths with controlled local storage
  and service interleavings, not live PostgreSQL concurrency acceptance.

Implemented:

- Activity reads now pass their SDK context to GORM. An already-canceled lookup
  or transition is rejected before SQL; cancellation after lookup prevents claim
  and proposal generation. The old context-free read method remains as an
  internal compatibility/test wrapper, not the activity's production read path.
- Activity code no longer calls repository whole-row Update/Save. One conditional
  UPDATE claims an unstarted dispatching/scheduled/uncertain record; predicates
  bind ID, owner, workflow ID/type, scheduled instant, prior status/freshness and
  absent completion/start. Only a confirmed one-row receipt reaches the effect.
- Settlement binds that same running claim's start/freshness and identity. Only
  status, start/completion, summary, result and freshness can change. It cannot
  insert a removed row or replace concurrent ownership/completion/results.
  No schema/data migration or automatically reclaimable lease was added.
- A running or already-started failed run requires review instead of blind
  execution. Lost claim/settlement or uncertain proposal outcome uses fixed SDK
  `GovernedActivityNeedsReview`, marked non-retryable. The actual SDK virtual
  workflow test confirms an ambiguous completion does not trigger more effect
  calls under the existing retry policy.
- Cancellation immediately after confirmed claim records a failed-before-effect
  summary without proposal generation. After the proposal service is entered,
  outcome settlement uses a separate three-second bounded context even when the
  caller has canceled. Confirmed completed replay does not regenerate proposals.
- Nil proposal results require review instead of panic/false success. Private
  injected storage error details do not enter the fixed SDK failure payload.
- README and planner guide document state/retry changes, conditional ownership,
  cancellation limits and the need to drain old unconditional-write workers
  before rollout. Existing history/rows were not reset or reauthorized.

Verification and reviewed failures:

- First complete temporalbridge run passed after the main source change.
- Expanded/final-named runs subsequently failed the new SQL assertion because
  it omitted GORM's predicate parentheses and compared pointer timestamp binds
  with value/untyped-nil expectations. The dedicated binding inspection recorded
  the actual types. Expectations now match GORM's real query/bind shape while
  retaining all identity/status/claim constraints; failed evidence was retained.
- `production-low-resource-temporal-activity-claim-reviewed-oct02-20261001.receipt.json`:
  full temporalbridge, lifecycle, router and safety suites passed; terminal exit
  0, no timeout, native Windows Go 1.25.13, one worker, 384 MiB soft heap limit,
  offline dependencies, no integration tag/destructive database tests.
- Cases include two simultaneous pre-claim snapshots with one effect winner,
  an already-running duplicate, interrupted running/failed rows, cancellation
  before/after claim and after service return, uncertain completion writes,
  completed replay, nil service result, real SDK non-retry behavior and changed,
  completed or missing records during settlement.
- Actual GORM dry-run checks inspect claim/settlement UPDATE and parameter scope,
  exactly-one-row/error receipt mapping, invalid transition refusal and canceled
  context refusal before SQL. They use an unconnected pool, not PostgreSQL locks.
- All 88 local CI-contract checks passed. Focused whitespace checks passed with
  ordinary Windows line-ending warnings. No local race detector/remote CI run,
  browser acceptance, frontend rebuild, signed release or live provider proof.

Limits remain explicit: the synchronous `RunDueOpenLoopsForOwner` interface has
no context argument, so it is not forcibly interruptible once entered. Lifecycle
ownership is retained rather than abandoning its call. HAI ledger completion is
not independent proof that Temporal accepted workflow completion. The existing
open-loop transactional projection, process-level concurrency, PostgreSQL crash/
rollback, durable execution authorization, binding to SDK execution metadata,
legacy approval provenance and operator recovery remain acceptance gates. These
conditional statements are not an exactly-once or cross-provider atomicity claim.

## Contextual Follow-Up Service And Transaction Wiring

On 2026-10-02 the parent resumed the production goal with no new agents,
containers, downloads, live/private database tests, migrations, deployments,
commits or pushes. The user's protected Joyce and ShareT applications stayed
running. This phase replaces the contextual activity/HTTP limitation recorded
above; it does not retroactively change those earlier test receipts.

### Reproduced Faults And Retained Intermediate Evidence

- `production-low-resource-followup-context-baseline-oct02-20261001` failed two
  actual activity-boundary cases: the contextual adapter was never called,
  while the legacy method was called once; an unsupported legacy-only adapter
  was claimed/executed and incorrectly completed.
- `production-low-resource-followup-http-context-baseline-oct02-20261001` failed
  actual handler cases: missing owner, malformed JSON and a wrong JSON field type
  returned HTTP 200 and called the legacy service; the valid request lost its
  cancellation context.
- The expanded native run found an existing nil-pointer crash in
  `followUpRootDB` when a nonnil GORM handle lacked its embedded configuration.
  The root guard now checks that configuration before dereferencing its dialect.
- The emergency-boundary baseline returned success and wrote another audit
  event after cancellation during a recorded stop decision. The contextual loop
  now stops before that next write and returns the cancellation error without
  discarding the already recorded decision.
- Intermediate startup-test receipts contain a test compile error from wrong
  status field names, not behavioral startup evidence. They remain retained.
  The reviewed intermediate also found a panic introduced by pointer binding
  for a JSON `null` request through the installed Gin validator. This was fixed
  using the standard JSON decoder with explicit nonnull-object validation;
  tests were not removed or weakened to accept `null` as an empty request.

### Integrated Production Behavior

- `workflow.ContextualFollowUpService` is a separate capability, not a new
  method silently added to the legacy embedded `Service` interface. The actual
  canonical service implements it. Temporal checks it before worker connection/
  startup and before a claim, and calls it with the SDK activity context.
- The canonical method requires a nonblank owner and a live context. Its private
  repository capability returns a detached GORM `WithContext` session. Existing
  due-loop queries, conditional claims, transactional projection and conditional
  release all use this same session. There is no context-free repository fallback,
  root-session mutation, copied active task-run `sync.Map`, extra background
  execution system, new data store or schema change.
- Cancellation is checked after lookup/claim, before projection, after returned
  outcomes and before each next loop. A claim whose acknowledgement is interrupted
  is not assumed absent or blindly released. A canceled transaction outcome stays
  reviewable. A confirmed durable proposal/checklist/decision remains recorded;
  cancellation cannot roll it back by changing a later summary.
- Advisory life-graph projection receives the execution context. Cancellation
  prevents a subsequent graph/audit call and cannot erase the already durable
  workflow projection. Its graph still grants no execution/approval authority.
- The HTTP follow-up endpoint requires the verified owner, a valid nonnull JSON
  object and the contextual capability. Missing adapters return 503 without
  executing; invalid/missing-owner requests return 400/401 before execution.
  A nil result cannot be reported as successful completion. Existing private
  error redaction remains fixed-message rather than returning storage secrets.
- Native regressions use the actual workflow service with controlled storage to
  stop at lookup, claim, transaction, confirmed commit, graph and emergency audit
  boundaries. They preserve owner isolation, committed results and outstanding
  claim identity, and prevent another due loop from being claimed after stop.
  Actual GORM statement callbacks inspect separate worker contexts on lookup,
  update and refetch. A nonconnecting pool exercises GORM transaction entry for
  both commit and release, checking cancellation/error/context propagation.

- No local race detector, remote CI, current browser/operator acceptance,

Cooperative contexts are not forced goroutine termination. The storage probes

## Workflow Scheduler Context And Stage Safety Integration

### Observed Failures And Retained Baselines

The initial `scheduler-context-baseline-oct02` native run failed to compile
because the new test used `WorkerID` instead of the existing `LockedBy` model
field. This was a test defect, not a behavioral baseline. Its evidence remains.

After correcting that field, `scheduler-context-behavior-baseline-oct02` reached
the actual ticker startup and durable recurring handler through
`durablejob.Runner`. Both continued through legacy follow-up and task execution
after cancellation at recovery or follow-up. A safety gate changed during
recovery also allowed the durable handler to continue and acknowledge success.
The queue storage was controlled in memory, not a PostgreSQL acceptance test.

The first repair passed those cases. Additional
`scheduler-stage-failure-baseline-oct02` cases then exposed continued execution
after returned cancellation, missing contextual capability or explicit deferral
errors, plus an unredacted credential-shaped value in the aggregated error.
These failed logs were retained rather than replaced with passing receipts.

### Implemented Wiring And Verification Scope

- Ticker and durable scheduler now invoke one shared sweep using their actual
  execution context. Missing trusted contextual follow-up capability is refused
  before any sweep stage, and before durable recurring registration.
- `ContextualFollowUpBatchService` is separate from the owner-scoped HTTP/Temporal
  capability. It uses the same detached GORM session, projection/claim machinery
  and existing follow-up transaction. No alternate executor was introduced;
  owner validation was not relaxed for authenticated callers.
- Context cancellation and background permission are checked between stages and
  after the final task stage. Returned cancellation, missing-context capability
  and deferral errors stop subsequent stages. A safety pause is deferred by the
  actual runner without consuming the attempt budget in the controlled queue.
- Ordinary stage errors remain aggregated with their original Go error identity.
  Their rendered sweep error redacts recognized credential patterns before the
  durable runner stores a retry failure. This is not universal PII or secret
  detection, nor a claim that all other durable job handlers sanitize errors.
- Actual batch-service tests cover two owners, invalid/canceled context,
  unsupported repositories and cancellation after an uncertain claim. Existing
  owner-isolation, transaction-context and confirmed-result tests still pass.
- Additional checks cover disabled follow-up compatibility, no legacy fallback,
  reminder/task cancellation boundaries, stop-error identity, ordinary-error
  aggregation and a redacted retry through the actual durable runner.

`output/production-low-resource-scheduler-context-accepted-native-oct02-20261001.receipt.json`.
It records 2026-10-02T01:41:47.3641235Z through 01:43:05.3015847Z, exit 0 and no
timeout for the full workflow, Temporal bridge, durable-job, lifecycle, router
and safety package suites. All 88 local CI-contract tests passed afterward.
Tracked-file whitespace checks passed with Windows line-ending warnings.

Verification stayed serial/offline with Go 1.25.13 Windows/amd64, CGO disabled,
one worker/one test parallelism, a 384 MiB soft heap target, read-only dependencies,
no integration build tag, no downloads and destructive database tests disabled.
No containers, agents, deployments, migrations, commits or pushes were started.

### Remaining Limits

Recovery, reminder-delivery and task APIs still do not take execution context
while entered; only their stage boundaries are covered by this change. A policy
pause between checkpoints cannot forcibly terminate an already-entered call.
Older ambient and agent-cycle entry points remain context-free and require
separate conversion. The compatibility methods remain available for those
existing callers; the revised workflow schedulers no longer use them.

Controlled queue/storage probes do not prove PostgreSQL lease serialization,
transaction rollback, interrupted locks, socket cancellation, crash/recovery or
cross-process behavior. No local race detector, remote CI, current browser,
frontend rebuild, live provider, signed installer or operator acceptance was run.
HAI remains stopped and Joyce/ShareT must remain running and untouched.
All original production gates below remain open; this does not complete HAI.

## Ambient Scan Context And Receipt Integration

### Scope And Observed Failures

Trusted ambient ticker/durable scheduling, the actual ambient service and GORM
repository, plus the owner HTTP scan acknowledgement path were inspected and
repaired. This is an additive source integration, not a running deployment.

Retained native behavioral baselines:

- `ambient-context-behavior-baseline-oct02`: actual ticker/runner handlers used
  legacy scanning instead of passing their owned context; nil scans could
  acknowledge success. Controlled queue storage was used, not a server.
- `ambient-outcome-ack-baseline-oct02`: a missing settlement repository caused a
  real service nil-pointer panic. That failure is retained separately.
- `ambient-outcome-nonpanic-baseline-oct02`: malformed terminal receipts were
  accepted, pruning continued, adapter mutations changed known results, and
  settlement errors lost uncertainty or storage-error identity.
- `ambient-creation-ack-baseline-oct02`: malformed creation receipts allowed
  context reads, proposal writes and terminal acknowledgements to continue.
- `ambient-storage-root-baseline-oct02`: dry-run and missing-dialect database
  roots were accepted as contextual execution storage.

### Integrated Behavior

- `ContextualScanService` is a separate trusted-system capability. Ticker and
  durable recurring handlers pass their owned execution context and the actual
  safety gate. Missing capability never falls back to legacy `Scan`.
- The service retains its original atomic admission guard and creates a narrow
  execution view over a detached GORM session. The guard/session are not copied
  into shared mutable state. Invalid/canceled contexts, missing storage,
  dry-run/missing/non-PostgreSQL dialects and borrowed transaction roots are
  refused before contextual scan work.
- Checkpoints stop new stages/proposal writes after cancellation or permission
  changes. Follow-ups use the contextual batch capability. Known partial
  follow-up/task results are recorded before handling a subsequent error.
- Scan IDs are assigned before creation. Creation and terminal receipts must
  match the expected ID, owner, trigger, state, timestamp and result fields.
  Microsecond timestamp precision is normalized; database-managed creation and
  update timestamps are excluded. Expected values and request completion-time
  pointers are snapshotted independently of mutable adapter arguments.
- Missing/mismatched receipts and storage errors produce `outcome_unconfirmed`,
  not success. Ambiguous completion is not followed by a speculative failed
  update. These service checks cover both trusted and owner-scoped scans.
- Failure-only settlement uses a separate two-second cooperative context after
  cancellation. It never resumes scanning. A missing settlement repository no
  longer panics. Original failure/storage error identities are retained, and
  recognized secret patterns are redacted from scan previews and rendered
  scheduler failure messages.
- Terminal GORM updates require exactly one still-running row matched by ID,
  owner and microsecond start time. No Save/upsert fallback can recreate a
  deleted scan or overwrite a newer terminal outcome.
- Retention only selects completed/failed rows. Personal retention is restricted
  to the owner; adapters without that capability skip retention rather than
  falling back to global history. No real database records were pruned here.

timeout for all ambient, workflow, Temporal bridge, durable-job, lifecycle,
router, safety and API-error package suites. All 88 local CI-contract tests
passed afterward. Tracked-file whitespace checks passed with line-ending
warnings, not source errors.

Coverage includes actual ticker/runner context wiring, cancellation checkpoints,
gate changes, known partial outcomes, independent GORM scopes/conditional SQL,
controlled affected-row acknowledgements, owner/terminal retention SQL, actual
HTTP-handler/service responses, actual runner/service acknowledgement and
recognized-secret redaction. The latter uses controlled queue/storage/principals,
not live IDP sessions or PostgreSQL server transactions. GORM DryRun retention
checks inspect generated SQL only and are not evidence of a completed deletion.

one worker/one test parallelism, a 384 MiB soft heap target, read-only dependencies,
no integration tag, no downloads and destructive database tests disabled.
No agents, Docker starts/builds, deployments, migrations, commits or pushes were
started. HAI remains stopped; Joyce and ShareT were not changed.

### Explicit Remaining Gates

- Owner HTTP scans and legacy `Scan`/agent-cycle callers remain context-free.
  Memory/pursuit reads and task execution do not accept scan context while
  entered. Checkpoints are cooperative boundaries, not forcible termination.
- At this receipt's cutoff the recurring runner still retried unconfirmed ambient failures. A joined
  safety-pause/settlement failure can still be classified as deferred, losing

  reason. Durable quarantine, reconciliation and safe recurrence admission for
  uncertain scans remained unfinished. The source quarantine work below

  The two-second context is not a forced bound on a driver that ignores context.

## Durable Ambient Review And Persisted Replay Policy

### Scope And Reproduced Defects

The current source closes acknowledged uncertainty admission and persisted
crash-replay gaps for trusted ambient scheduling. It does not declare the full
product production-ready or provide an operator review/resume workflow.

Retained red receipts reproduce actual runner/ambient-service and GORM behavior:

- `production-low-resource-durable-review-behavior-baseline-oct02`: unknown
  outcomes were ordinary successful polls; unacknowledged holds were not refused.
- `production-low-resource-ambient-review-chain-baseline-oct02`: an actual
  ambient service's unconfirmed and pause-plus-unconfirmed result could be
  retried/deferred, and scheduling admitted missing review persistence.
- `production-low-resource-durable-review-invalid-root-baseline-oct02`: a
  DryRun root was acknowledged and a missing GORM config could panic.
- `production-low-resource-review-crash-policy-red-oct02`: actual generated
  reaper SQL returned expired work to pending/dead without persisted replay
  eligibility or a quarantine update.
- `production-low-resource-review-policy-authority-red-oct02`: ordinary
  registration downgraded a successor's review policy, missing handlers quietly
  dead-lettered review-sensitive work, and explicit producers omitted policy.

The older `durable-review-sql-expanded` failure was a controlled Count fixture
missing RowsAffected; it is preserved and is not a production SQL defect.

### Source Integration

- `needs_review` is nonclaimable, counts as active for admission/replacement,
  and committed holds block pending same-queue/kind claims. Review classification
  wins over deferral, including false classifiers on another joined branch.
- Review writes use a bounded independent context and exact running-row ID,
  worker and lease-generation fences; nil/invalid/DryRun/non-PostgreSQL/borrowed
  transaction roots are refused. Recognized credentials are redacted, not
  represented as a claim of complete personal-data scrubbing.
- `RegisterReviewRecurring` requires explicit review and scheduling persistence.
  Registration upgrades existing active occurrences under the singleton lock
  before publishing a handler. Existing public producers and successor
  creation preserve the registered/persisted stricter policy. Missing handlers
  hold review-sensitive work without consuming a never-started attempt.
- Cancellation/deadline, heartbeat failure and panic after invocation require
  review. Failed hold writes return an error and leave the occurrence leased.
  Persisted `review_unknown` policy makes later lease recovery quarantine that
  occurrence, including missing lease timestamps and final attempts, even if
  its recurring schedule is absent. Only explicit `at_least_once` policy permits
  ordinary retry/dead recovery; invalid persisted policy fails closed.
- Ambient durable startup cannot silently fall back to a ticker. Explicit
  legacy ticker mode still has a process-local hold only, not a restart-safe
  production quarantine.
- New paired source migration `pre/0115_durable_job_replay_policy` adds/validates
  non-null replay policy, upgrades active ambient rows and indexes review holds.
  Rollback locks before checking and refuses any retained review/replay
  authority, including an unexpected null policy; it deletes no rows.

### Verification And Remaining Gates

Native verification is serial/offline Go 1.25.13 Windows, one worker/one test
parallelism, CGO disabled, a 384 MiB soft heap target, read-only dependencies,
no integration tag/downloads and destructive database tests disabled. This is
actual runner/service and GORM SQL/transport with controlled acknowledgements,
not real PostgreSQL transaction durability or a physical RAM cap.

`production-low-resource-review-policy-authority-checked-oct02` passed full
durablejob/ambient/migrations suites at 02:24:59.2989294-02:26:07.5031327 UTC.
The final source receipt for that earlier phase
`production-low-resource-review-crash-policy-final-source-oct02-20261001.receipt.json`
records all nine full native package suites passing: durablejob, ambient,
migrations, workflow, temporalbridge, lifecycle, router, safety and apierror;
exit 0, no timeout, 02:26:39.6807171-02:28:38.7432946 UTC on October 2.
After that verifier was terminal, `C:\Python314\python.exe
scripts/test_ci_contract.py` passed all 88 CI/source contract tests. Scoped
`git diff --check` returned 0 with only line-ending normalization warnings.
Dedicated PostgreSQL crash/restart, stale settlement, active-policy upgrade and
cross-queue claim-hold acceptance definitions were added but not executed.

Still required: actual SQL concurrency/commit/crash/rollback and cancellation
acceptance; serialization against a claim already racing a hold; uncertain

## Acknowledged Settlement And Confirmed Recurrence Preparation

### Reproduced Failures

The retained `settlement-ack-baseline-oct02` runner baseline reproduces false
successful polls when outcome writes return `false, nil`, recognized secret
leakage from settlement errors, loss of the original failure after a failed
review hold, and a review hold incorrectly applied to a handler whose lifecycle
admission was refused before launch.

`settlement-commit-admission-baseline-oct02` independently reproduces actual
GORM transaction commit failure returning `owned=true` and active-count SQL
omitting preparation holds. Neither red receipt has been overwritten.

The later `review-settlement-legacy-policy-red-oct02` SQL baseline reproduces
ordinary completion lacking a persisted replay-policy and queue/kind fence.
Rejecting only a caller-supplied review-policy successor was insufficient: a
stale caller could settle a row after its policy was upgraded. That failed
baseline is also preserved.

### Current Source Integration

- All runner outcome writes inspect acknowledgement as well as error. Refused
  writes cannot become successful polls. Review-sensitive failures attempt a
  bounded conditional hold; original and storage error identities are retained
  while recognized credentials are redacted from rendered messages.
- Refused lifecycle admission releases only an unstarted claim, without an
  attempt increment. A failed release is still uncertain, not successful
  shutdown. Already-invoked work remains subject to review policy.
- Ordinary recurring transaction errors now return `false, false, err`, not
  tentative in-transaction acknowledgements. Ordinary completion additionally
  requires the stored row's explicit `at_least_once` policy and matching queue/
  kind, so supplying an ordinary successor cannot downgrade a review occurrence.
- Review-sensitive completion uses contextual independent-root GORM sessions
  and two conditional transactions. Confirmed preparation retains worker/lease
  identity, writes nonterminal `settling`, and inserts at most one pending
  successor under the same queue/kind singleton advisory lock. Release requires
  exact preparation identity, attempts, microsecond freshness, reason and absent
  completion. Generic legacy completion refuses a review-policy successor.
- `settling` is nonclaimable, counted active by admission/startup/replacement,
  and blocks already-pending same-queue/kind claims. It is never automatically
  reaped. The unshipped/unapplied 0115 index and rollback guard now also preserve
  this status. No live schema or historic row has been modified.
- Lost preparation acknowledgement or cancellation before release retains the
  barrier if preparation committed. A lost release acknowledgement can still
  mean release committed based on confirmed preparation. Operator inspection
  and authorized same-record reconciliation remain required; this is not
  exactly-once execution or a completed recovery protocol.
- Review registration requires explicit hold, policy-admission and contextual
  recurring-completion capabilities before handler publication or enqueue.

### Verification Scope

`production-low-resource-review-settlement-native-first-oct02-20261001.receipt.json`
passed full durablejob/ambient/migrations native suites at
02:43:18.4639389-02:44:09.6028500 UTC on October 2, exit 0 with no timeout.
Real GORM/database/sql calls over a scripted transport exercise both commits,
lost acknowledgement at either commit, canceled release admission, rejected
preparation/release rows, existing successor admission, exact SQL fences,
secret redaction, invalid roots/inputs and missing adapter capability. These
are controlled transport tests, not PostgreSQL transaction-isolation proof.

PostgreSQL definition commits through a real transaction before injecting a

and exact successor claims. It has not been executed. The existing literal
dedicated database and destructive-test opt-in guard remains required.

The first nine-package consumer receipt, `review-settlement-consumer-final-oct02`,
passed at 02:45:46.6440477-02:47:46.2961366 UTC before the final legacy-policy
SQL fence was added. It is historical evidence, not acceptance of later source.

## Owned Internal Reminder Cancellation And Request Admission

### Source scope

The canonical reminder route previously rebuilt transaction/sink context from
`context.Background()` and the HTTP/ticker/durable scheduler invoked legacy
context-free delivery methods. Internal delivery could therefore continue
without the initiating request's or worker's cancellation. This phase preserves
the exact separately owner-approved authorization and locked evidence checks;
it changes execution ownership, not external-sending authority.

- New owner-scoped HTTP and separate trusted system-wide scheduling capabilities
  create a contextual GORM view without mutating shared storage or copying the
  service's active-task `sync.Map`. The contextual service requires atomic
  reminder storage; the explicit legacy memory-fixture marker is not a bypass.
- Due reads, per-authorization PostgreSQL transactions, revalidation and the
  existing transactional internal proactivity sink retain caller context. The
  transaction's 30-second cooperative timeout now inherits its parent rather
  than replacing it. Invalid/canceled contexts are refused before storage.
- Checkpoints after lookup/revalidation/sink and before receipt/next delivery
  stop more work after cancellation. Cancellation returns to the existing
  signal-plus-receipt transaction as an error, not a synthetic retry receipt.
  Confirmed prior delivery counts remain in an interrupted batch's summary.
- The workflow sweep refuses a missing reminder contextual capability before
  recurring-job registration or earlier recovery mutation. Disabled reminders
  do not interfere with other enabled stages. A nil reminder outcome stops the
  sweep rather than allowing task execution.
- HTTP requires a verified owner and one bounded JSON object, rejecting null,
  empty, malformed, trailing and oversized requests and limits outside 0-100
  before service invocation. Zero retains the existing service default. Missing
  contextual capability returns 503 with no legacy fallback; failed/missing
  outcomes have fixed public error text rather than private storage details.
  The existing frontend still sends the same JSON request contract.

### Reproductions retained

- `reminder-stop-context-red-oct02` reproduced detached transaction context,
  context-free scheduled/HTTP execution and unsafe/missing-result HTTP outcomes.
- `reminder-context-validation-red-oct02` reproduced acceptance of trailing JSON,
  trailing garbage, oversized input, and negative/excessive limits. The actual
  service cancellation/partial-confirmation boundary probes already passed.
- `reminder-context-boundaries-first-oct02` exposed an incorrect new test
  assumption that the owner was the final SQL argument. The assertion now binds
  the owner predicate's actual PostgreSQL placeholder to its argument instead
  of confusing it with the later LIMIT argument. No production scope was
  weakened to accommodate that fixture.
- `reminder-context-final-native-oct02` passed eight package suites but failed
  the router permission fixture: it sent no JSON while expecting to reach the
  absent-service boundary. Valid authorized requests now use `{}` and retain
  the 503 expectation; empty/null/trailing/excessive authorized requests are
  separately asserted as 400. Viewer/unknown roles remain forbidden. This failed
  receipt is historical, not final-source acceptance.

## Owned Atomic Claim Recovery And Confirmed History

### Reproduced boundary failures

The retained red receipt
`output/production-low-resource-recovery-context-red-oct02-20261001.receipt.json`
records exit 1, no timeout, 03:08:26.8945131-03:08:49.4657766 UTC on October 2.
Its actual handler/scheduler/transaction-entry probes reproduced legacy
context-free invocation, missing owners and invalid/null/trailing JSON entering
recovery, nil results reported as success, and missing atomic repository methods.
The old recovery service also treated mutation failures as skipped results and
did not require acknowledgement of separately written history.

### Implemented boundaries

- Authenticated owner and trusted system-wide recovery have separate contextual
  capabilities. Scoped GORM sessions inherit the owned context without copying
  the active-task map or modifying shared storage. Dry-run, missing/non-PostgreSQL
  dialects and borrowed transaction roots are refused for canonical recovery.
- Canonical claim recovery owns one read-committed transaction with a 30-second
  cooperative child timeout. Workflow state plus transition/decision/event, or
  open-loop state plus decision/event, must be acknowledged together. Each
  history insert requires one affected row. Failure or lost commit acknowledgement
  returns no confirmed per-claim outcome; it is not counted as a harmless skip.
- Recovery locks the workflow before the loop, matches the candidate revision
  and claim, binds owner scope and rejects archived parents. Lease authority is
  `clock_timestamp()` read after the locks, not the supplied ahead PC clock.
  Unknown workflow effects remain blocked for review with the claim fence retained.
  Only existing idempotent internal follow-up claims reopen; no external retry,
  approval or sending authority is created by this change.
- Cancellation is rechecked between reads and claims. Previously acknowledged
  results remain available in interrupted batch summaries. Unconfirmed mutation
  identity stops the scheduler before follow-ups or task execution. Life-graph
  projection remains advisory and is attempted only after acknowledged commit.
- Recovery HTTP requires a verified owner and one JSON object bounded to 4 KiB;
  null/malformed/trailing/oversized input and limits outside 0-50 are rejected
  before service invocation. Zero retains the service default. Missing adapters
  return 503; storage/nil-result failures use fixed errors without private detail.
  Scheduler registration and invocation refuse a missing contextual capability;
  no legacy recovery fallback is permitted at those boundaries.
- Canonical GORM legacy recovery callers also use the atomic storage path, but
  their compatibility entry creates a background context. This does not claim
  owned caller cancellation for old agent-cycle callers or entered task/runtime
  execution. Older in-memory compatibility fixtures are not production storage.

failed/unacknowledged evidence writes, commit uncertainty, live leases and
archived parents. These are offline acknowledgements, not actual PostgreSQL

## Entered Task, Review And Chat Cancellation (October 2)

This section records current source integration, not deployment, real-provider
acceptance or full-product production readiness. It extends the preceding
owned recovery work without removing its historical evidence or open gates.
Joyce and ShareT remain protected; HAI stays stopped. Verification remains
serial and offline, without new agents, containers, downloads or paid calls.

### Reproduced failures and retained evidence

- `production-low-resource-task-cancel-admission-red-oct02-20261001.receipt.json`
  retained the original cancelled task plan/run HTTP admission failure.
- `production-low-resource-task-execution-context-first-native-oct02-20261001.receipt.json`
  retained the first broad failure. The local provider fixture lacked the
  required project key; the scheduler/adapter fixtures lacked the new trusted
  capability or required workflow ID. Required authority and identifiers were
  preserved, not weakened to make the fixtures pass. The subsequent three-suite
  `task-execution-context-second-native-oct02` receipt passed, but was not treated
  as final proof of later source changes.
- `production-low-resource-review-and-workflow-admission-red-oct02-20261001.receipt.json`
  retained actual HTTP failures: cancelled approval entered resolution, and
  null/trailing/oversized execution envelopes entered workflow execution.
- `production-low-resource-entered-task-and-reviewed-context-final-native-oct02-20261001.receipt.json`
  retained the later broad router failure: its legacy test adapter lacked the
  contextual review method. The adapter was updated; owner-only mutation
  permission assertions and refusal of legacy production adapters stayed intact.
- `production-low-resource-assistant-lifetime-red-oct02-20261001.receipt.json`
  retained the chat handler/service failure to forward its owned task lifetime.

All these failed receipts reported exit 1 without timeout. They remain alongside
successful receipts; neither observation timeouts nor partial package passes
were substituted for a completed, successful verifier.

### Implemented chain

- Authenticated task plan/run, workflow execution and chat commands forward a
  trusted nonserialized context. It does not change source/intent digests, owner
  authority or reviewed approval. JSON cannot supply a caller execution context.
- Workflow HTTP and scheduled execution require contextual capabilities;
  direct runner capability is checked before claiming work. Deferred runners
  refuse legacy delegates before dispatch. The owner due-run route requires one
  non-null JSON object bounded to 4 KiB, no trailing values and limit 0-50.
- Preview, task execution, model routing/generation and controlled automation
  launch inherit the caller lifetime. Cancellation checkpoints stop new stages,
  validation fallback and automatic retry. Received model usage and runtime
  receipts are retained for review instead of being erased or called completion.
- Entered synchronous runners remain registered and leased until they actually
  return. A cancellation signal does not falsely terminate an uncooperative
  worker, release its active-task registration or allow the batch to continue.
- Review HTTP requires contextual owner resolution; it never falls back to a
  context-free resolver. Pre-cancelled requests cannot record a decision. A
  committed approval remains immutable; execution gets this caller's lifetime
  rather than an old serialized context. Interrupted approved work requires
  reconciliation and links any returned result to the review. Outcome write
  errors/missing receipts cannot masquerade as confirmed updates. Original
  execution and review-outcome errors retain their identities together.
- Chat preserves returned task evidence on failure, does not start a subsequent
  maintenance cycle after observed cancellation, and does not log successful
  command completion for that interrupted path. Fixed HTTP errors distinguish
  cancellation, deadline expiry and required reconciliation without disclosing
  private engine diagnostics. Pursuit/agent-cycle calls themselves are still
  context-free while entered; these checkpoints do not pretend otherwise.

## Owned Task Operation Storage Context (October 2)

The prior evidence-retention fix did not bound entered task-state SQL. The
new red receipt `production-low-resource-operation-storage-context-red-oct02-20261001`
records missing PostgreSQL context capability and missing operation-admission
scoping (exit 1, no timeout). The final fixture uses a valid 64-character digest
to reach the actual GORM transaction path; the initial capability refusal occurred
before that path. No live PostgreSQL connection was made.

Implementation:

- Canonical `PostgresTaskStateRepository.WithTaskStateContext` creates a detached
  GORM scope. It refuses nil, dry-run, non-PostgreSQL and borrowed transaction
  roots instead of claiming an independent commit on a caller-owned transaction.
- Owned operation claim/replay, terminal completion/readback and reconciliation
  use fresh contexts with a 30-second storage deadline. Already failed/cancelled
  execution gets independently bounded reconciliation via `WithoutCancel`.
  Received evidence and execution/storage error identities remain preserved.
- Heartbeats use their own owned context while the actual execution remains live.
  Each write is bounded; stop cancels an entered cooperative write and joins the
  heartbeat goroutine. Caller cancellation alone does not end an active runtime.
- Owned requests refuse missing storage-context adapters before claim/execution.
  HTTP returns a fixed 503 without private storage details. Legacy context-free
  callers remain compatible and do not receive universal cancellation guarantees.
- Repository-specific log/review helpers retain shared service mirrors and mutexes;
  no shared service or root repository context is overwritten or copied.

Offline tests cover actual GORM transaction context transport, an entered
transaction receiving deadline expiry, unchanged root context, rejected storage
roots, fail-closed legacy admission, safe HTTP errors, and heartbeat-stop
cancellation/join. The memory repository has no external transport and its scoped
admission is not proof of interruptible mutex acquisition.

## Owned Review Decision And Outcome Storage (October 2)

Review resolution previously checked cancellation but used context-free root
storage for review lookup, prior-plan inspection, immutable decision insertion
and outcome recording. The retained red receipt
`production-low-resource-review-storage-context-red-oct02-20261001` records
missing scoped reads/decisions/outcomes for rejection and cancelled runtime
execution (exit 1, no timeout). Its detached memory views check actual service
wiring; they do not exercise live PostgreSQL.

The owned resolver now uses `taskOperationStorage` for admission reads and
decision storage, with caller context and a 30-second deadline. It releases the
admission scope after decision acknowledgement, before execution. Outcome
recording uses a fresh `WithoutCancel` context with the same deadline. A failed
or missing outcome acknowledgement retains the acknowledged item and received
task result, reports reconciliation-required failure, and does not forge an
updated mirror. Cancellation after rejection preserves that decision and prevents
correction learning from starting. Missing contextual storage is refused before
decision/execution; the real review handler returns a fixed, private-safe 503.
No shared service/mutex or root repository context is copied or replaced.

`review_storage_context_test.go` covers rejection, cancellation during runtime,
cancellation after an acknowledged rejection, failure of outcome storage after
received execution, missing contextual storage before approval, and the actual
HTTP error path. It checks deadlines/context values, no dead caller context for
outcome storage, retained execution evidence, unchanged approved status after a
failed outcome write, and no new decision/tool call on refused admission.

## Owned Action-Approval Requirement Inspection (October 2)

reproduced owned execution using the legacy requirement reader, ignoring the

a bounded child context and no legacy fallback. The canonical service uses its
contextual configuration reader, checks cancellation before and after the read,

or prove that configuration stayed unchanged until dispatch.

## Owned Approval Proof Consumption (October 2)

`production-low-resource-approval-consumption-context-red-oct02-20261001.log`
reproduced cancellation before/during/after storage being accepted as consumed
authority, unbounded service/SQL contexts, borrowed SQL roots being accepted,
memory consumption ignoring cancellation and unexpected SQL row counts being
classified as replay. This was an assertion failure (exit 1), not a compiler
failure or timeout; the evidence remains retained.

Proof verification and consumption now have a bounded caller context. Cancellation
is checked before storage and after its response. Storage errors and cancelled
acknowledgements retain their original error identity together with
`ErrApprovalProofConsumptionUnconfirmed`. A possibly spent proof is not restored;
a follow-up attempt still encounters replay rejection. Ordinary duplicate proof
claims retain `ErrApprovalProofConsumed` rather than inventing storage uncertainty.

The PostgreSQL store uses the shared detached-root guard and refuses dry-run,
borrowed, missing or unsupported roots before entering SQL. A write affected-row
count of zero means duplicate; one means acknowledged claim; other counts require
reconciliation, not replay classification. The shared root context is unchanged.
The local memory store checks cancellation before entry and after acquiring its
mutex. This does not make mutex waiting forcibly interruptible or memory storage
production-durable. No proof-consumption records are rolled back by cancellation.

Fifteen new boundaries cover service cancellation/error/replay retention, direct
memory cancellation and controlled SQL scope/error/row-count handling. All six
full local suites passed without timeout:
`production-low-resource-approval-consumption-context-green-oct02-20261001.receipt.json`.
The cmd/task/automation entrypoint and integration definitions compiled with

retained in this task's terminal evidence; it is not a compiler failure.

A shared result validator now gates both key removal and Command Center success
presentation/timestamp updates. It requires matching automation identity, a
nonzero outcome UUID, exact completed/ready status and requiresApproval=false.
Unknown, missing, failed, nonterminal and mismatched results retain the original
key. Ready is presented as ready, not started. Existing compare-before-remove
behavior prevents an older acknowledgement from removing a replacement key.
No client key is deleted merely because transport returned HTTP success.

The native regression runs the actual service and RxJS pipeline with controlled
HTTP/localStorage substitutes, including reuse across new service instances.
Ten non-definitive/malformed cases retain the key; ready/completed cases clear it,
and an out-of-order acknowledgement retains a newer key. The new regression is
wired into the standard frontend test script. Existing Jasmine fixtures now bind
their returned automation/outcome IDs correctly; the automation service spec is
included as the fifth focused spec root. These Jasmine fixtures were typechecked,
not executed in a rendered browser in this phase.

## Bounded Launch HTTP Request Parsing (October 2)

The in-process launch-handler regression demonstrated that two JSON objects,
trailing literals, malformed unknown-length bodies, null and oversized bodies
reached the service. A valid unknown-length body lost its task/project/request
context because the prior parser gated all reading on Content-Length. Actual
assertion failures, not a compiler failure, are retained in
`production-low-resource-launch-request-http-red-oct02-20261001.log`.

The launch handler now uses the existing workflow-style standard JSON decoder
pattern with MaxBytesReader: optional empty body or exactly one object, a 64 KiB
whole-body bound and EOF confirmation after the first object. Parsing does not
depend on positive Content-Length. Null/arrays, malformed content and trailing
values return 400 before service entry; oversized content returns 413. Error
responses contain a fixed message, not supplied body content. Client authority
fields remain excluded by the existing binding contract; idempotency header/body
conflict checks and server-owned approval/identity handling remain intact.

Twelve controlled cases cover eight invalid bodies (including oversized trailing
whitespace), valid unknown-length context and empty/object/exact-limit bodies
with zero declared length. The service probe records admission only; these new
tests invoke no external action. Proxy transfer/read-timeout, slow-client and
actual deployment acceptance remain separate from in-process handler proof.

no SQL cases:
`production-low-resource-launch-request-http-entrypoint-oct02-20261001.receipt.json`.

## Outstanding Production Gates


1. Broader optional-store coverage and additional crash/recovery acceptance.
   Current backend, Windows/Linux IDP and the 13 new real-store session cases
   pass; optional skips remain separately reported, not counted as integration proof.
2. A PostgreSQL snapshot recovery fence outside restored authority. Durable rows
   stop Redis-only rollback from reviving authority; they do not solve rollback
   of those same PostgreSQL rows.
3. Accept the new pre-dispatch uncertainty/atomic-mutation changes against actual
   PostgreSQL crash and rollback scenarios, and implement cross-process filesystem
   identity reconciliation. Source and synthetic test definitions are not crash
   acceptance or an exactly-once guarantee.
   The durable feed registry is now wired, but its dedicated PostgreSQL test,
   interrupted-claim operator recovery and actual crash/restore remain unaccepted.
4. Trusted participant capability probes, actual task-bound reviewer dispatch and
   durable acknowledgements. Editable health declarations are now unverified,
   not replaced by fake successful probes or policy exemptions.
5. Production model/RLS, grants, pgvector, application and session recovery

## Runtime stop record provenance (October 2)

The stop service now rechecks non-zero record identity and exact record kind
against the lookup used. Pending-intent fallback additionally requires pending
status. An inconsistent record produces an indeterminate audit result without
entering cancellation admission. Valid active outcomes and pending intents
retain their existing stop behavior.

evidence, not PostgreSQL, runtime-provider or cross-process acceptance.

terminated only after checking its exact command and parent process identity;
current container health was not reverified. Stop-path contextual storage,
dispatch lifetime and audit durability acceptance remain open.

## Runtime stop replay receipt identity (October 2)

Stop replay now requires a non-zero outcome UUID and the same normalized
execution reference as the persisted stop intent, in addition to the existing
owner, automation, runtime, task, kind and event-key checks. An inconsistent
receipt returns an error without replaying it or issuing another cancellation.
Matching empty references remain valid for the direct CLI path. Missing outcomes
continue to report the unresolved intent, not a successful stop.

## Reference-stop adapter acknowledgement binding (October 2)

The runtime registry no longer overwrites a reference-stop adapter response's
execution reference with the requested value. Both locally tracked and
restart/untracked reference-stop paths compare the returned runtime, task and
reference. A mismatch returns a fresh indeterminate result tied to the requested
identity, discards the unbound response payload and does not repeat cancellation.
The adapter may already have acted; this is not proof that no effect occurred.

and owner-bound-without-reference adapter interfaces were not changed by this
patch. Stop context propagation, actual storage durability and operator

## Other registry stop-route acknowledgements (October 2)

The owner-bound tracked/untracked stop routes and exact OpenClaw receipt route
now use the same acknowledgement identity guard as reference-stop dispatch.
Owner-bound dispatch validates runtime and task, preserving an adapter-discovered
reference because no expected reference was supplied. Exact-receipt dispatch
also compares the expected execution reference. Invalid responses become fresh
indeterminate results without forwarding the unbound message or audit payload
and without repeating dispatch. This guard cannot undo an already performed
adapter effect or prove remote cancellation.

## Pursuits runtime-stop presentation and request lifecycle (October 2)

The actual Pursuits component no longer treats every HTTP-success stop result
as a successful stop. Requested cancellation is informational; blocked and
uncertain results are warnings, failed results are errors. A stopped response
is presented as a stored report only with matching runtime, non-empty task and
a structurally valid non-zero launch-event evidence URI. This structural UI
check is not an independent durable task/owner verification. Raw provider
messages, invalid evidence URIs and error payloads are not copied into notices.

Stop subscriptions take one response, release busy state even on empty
completion/error/unsubscribe and unsubscribe on route destruction. The existing
busy guard prevents duplicate clicks during a request. No automatic retry was
introduced; uncertain responses direct the operator to review evidence.

Four tests run the actual component class with real RxJS and Angular forms.
After fixing harness compiler loading/class-field policy to match the app,
all four groups failed on actual pre-repair behavior: false success notices,
malformed-response handling, stuck busy state and post-destroy notifications.
All now pass and are included in the normal frontend test command.
The whole native frontend suite passed (53 tests), strict Angular template/app
compilation passed (five root files, now including Pursuits), spec TypeScript
passed (five roots), and all 89 CI contract checks passed. No output was emitted

visual review and real runtime cancellation remain unverified in this phase.
No containers or agents were started and Joyce/ShareT were untouched.

## Runtime stop failure summary configuration preservation (October 2)

Stop failure auditing no longer calls the full configuration Save/Update with
a previously read automation snapshot. The required repository method
`UpdateRuntimeStopFailure` changes only `last_failure_reason`, scoped to the
automation ID and guarded against a later `last_launch_at`. It does not replace
host, target, runtime settings or the last launch timestamp. Missing storage,
dry-run storage, nil IDs and zero start times cannot claim a successful update.
A zero-row update checks record existence; a newer launch can supersede the
summary without suppressing the durable stop audit event.

Summary update failure retains the stop audit event and records a fixed public
audit explanation instead of logging raw database details. It does not mutate
the borrowed configuration or infer cancellation success. This projection
guard does not serialize concurrent stop/health summaries or prove database
durability; stop context propagation and real PostgreSQL acceptance remain open.

`production-low-resource-stop-failure-projection-red-20261002-20261001.log`.

bindings. This is not live PostgreSQL proof.

## Runtime stop failure summary storage context (October 2)

The narrow failure-summary repository update now derives a bounded child
context from its existing GORM statement context. It preserves inherited
values/shorter deadlines and does not mutate the shared repository context.
Missing statement context or malformed storage is rejected without inventing
a background root. Cancellation is checked before writing and after the
driver returns; existence readback uses the same scope and checks cancellation
again. A driver success after cancellation cannot acknowledge the summary.

## Owned HTTP runtime stop request context (October 2)

The HTTP stop handler now passes the original request context and verified
actor to the context-aware service. Owned configuration reads, admission
lookups and intent storage use bounded contextual repositories without a
shared-root fallback. Cancellation is checked around storage acknowledgments;
an uncertain intent acknowledgment preserves its candidate evidence reference
without dispatching cancellation. Runtime dispatch receives the original
request context. Post-effect audit persistence uses a separate bounded scope
that retains request values but can survive request cancellation. Replay and
audit helpers accept explicit repositories without copying the service/mutex.
Legacy non-HTTP wrappers remain available and have not been migrated.

## DeepSeek explicit stop versus enqueue recovery context (October 2)

DeepSeek host cancellation no longer silently replaces a nil or cancelled
stop request with a background context. Such requests are blocked before
dispatcher access. A live request retains its values and receives the existing
bounded cancellation timeout. Cancellation during the dispatcher call yields
an indeterminate acknowledgement, even if the dispatcher reports success;
the message explicitly allows that persistence may already have occurred.
The original exact job reference is preserved and no automatic retry occurs.

Enqueue-overlap recovery remains a separate existing-effect cleanup path:
it explicitly detaches cancellation while retaining parent context values,
then uses the same bounded revocation helper. A nil recovery parent cannot
invent a new background root. This retains durable cleanup after an enqueue
race instead of disabling it as a side effect of request safety.

Four genuine pre-repair failures are preserved in
`production-low-resource-deepseek-stop-context-red-20261002-20261001.log`:
nil request dispatch, cancelled request dispatch, late success acknowledgement
and lost cleanup context values. Five cases now include a valid stop and
cover those failures. All seven suites passed in
`production-low-resource-deepseek-stop-context-green-20261002-20261001.log`.
All 89 CI contract checks passed; scoped whitespace checks passed.
This is local synthetic dispatcher proof, not actual host worker or PostgreSQL
commit/cancellation/crash acceptance. Production DSH execution remains gated
by the existing isolation and worker-authentication requirements.
No agents, containers, downloads, migrations, deployments, commits or pushes
were performed. Joyce and ShareT were untouched.

The adapter checks returned host-job identity before adopting a job reference

## Host service cancellation request scope (October 2)

The central host Service.CancelTask no longer invents a background context
for nil requests. It rejects nil/cancelled requests before repository access,
creates a bounded child using AdmissionTimeout, retains request values and
shorter deadlines, and checks cancellation after repository acknowledgment.
A late acknowledgment returns any candidate job with revoked=false and a
joined context/driver error rather than claiming durable cancellation.
No automatic cancellation retry was introduced.

The lightweight verifier initially rejected hostruntime at parameter
validation, before executing tests. Its package allowlist now includes that
module without changing memory, serial-execution or deadline limits.

`production-low-resource-host-stop-context-red2-20261002-20261001.log`.
The cases cover unbounded context, nil, cancelled-before-call, shorter parent
deadline, late acknowledgment and combined driver/cancellation failure.
The first broad post-repair run found a handler fixture passing nil context;
the fixture now explicitly supplies a valid context while retaining its
assertion that active execution is not prematurely reported as terminated.
That failed run is retained. All eight suites passed in
`production-low-resource-host-stop-context-green2-20261002-20261001.log`.
All 89 CI contract tests passed separately; scoped diff/gofmt checks passed.

This is local service/synthetic repository evidence. Direct GORM repository
nil-context fallbacks, protocol scope helpers and true PostgreSQL lock/commit/
rollback/cancel/crash acceptance remain open. A candidate returned after an

## Direct GORM host cancellation context (October 2)

The host GORM CancelTask repository no longer substitutes a background context
for a missing request. It rejects missing/malformed database bindings, dry-run
storage, missing context, invalid owner/task and zero operation time before
transaction entry. Live requests use an AdmissionTimeout child context that
preserves values and shorter deadlines without changing the shared GORM root.
Cancellation checks surround lookup, mutation, readback and transaction return.
An uncertain late acknowledgment cannot return revoked=true; it retains any
candidate job and joins the context error with the driver/transaction error.
This is not an assertion that the transaction certainly did not commit.

Seven genuine pre-repair failures are retained in
`production-low-resource-host-stop-repository-context-red-20261002-20261001.log`.
Eight offline cases cover bounded entry, missing context, prior cancellation,
cancel during BeginTx, shorter deadline, dry-run, nil repository and nil DB.
The connection pool probes GORM's actual transaction entry and deliberately
refuses BeginTx before SQL execution; it proves neither commit nor rollback.
All eight suites passed in
`production-low-resource-host-stop-repository-context-green-20261002-20261001.log`.
All 89 CI contract checks and scoped whitespace checks passed separately.

PostgreSQL lock ordering, SQL mutation/readback cancellation, commit uncertainty,
rollback and crash recovery remain live acceptance gates. Other host repository
and protocol context fallbacks remain separate audit targets. No containers,
agents, downloads, migrations, deployments, commits or pushes were performed.
HAI was not restarted; Joyce and ShareT were untouched.

## Uncertain runtime stop HTTP and dashboard recovery (October 2)

Authorized stop errors now retain a minimal recovery object when the service
returns a valid task/runtime binding and canonical nonzero audit-event URI.
The payload contains the requested automation ID, candidate stop-event ID/URI,
normalized reported status, reconciliationRequired=true and retryAllowed=false.
It excludes provider messages, audit payloads, execution references and secrets.
The candidate wording deliberately does not assert that an uncertain write
persisted or that the task terminated. Malformed references are omitted.

The Pursuits error path shows only a matching automation/event/URI pair with
those exact recovery flags, marks it persistence-unconfirmed, refreshes the
selected record and warns against repeating cancellation before reconciliation.
It never renders raw HTTP/provider text or automatically submits another stop.
Invalid/foreign recovery objects retain the existing generic failure path.
The flag is recovery guidance, not a new server-side permission mechanism.

`production-low-resource-stop-http-recovery-red-20261002-20261001.log`.

regression initially failed on the missing candidate display; its six input

All 54 native frontend tests passed, with strict Angular app/template and spec
compilation across five roots each. All eight backend suites passed in

All 89 CI contract tests and scoped whitespace checks passed separately.

## DeepSeek cancellation runtime and pre-start state (October 2)

Returned host stop receipts must now identify the DeepSeek runtime as well as
the already checked owner/task/job. Missing or foreign runtime records cannot
be adopted. A cancelled/revoked record cannot confirm pre-start revocation
while it retains a lease digest, lease expiry, start-intent ID or actual-start
acknowledgment. Those results remain indeterminate and retain only correctly
bound evidence; no repeat dispatch is attempted. The success wording now says
before execution start, not before confirmation: confirmation and actual start
are different protocol stages.

Already-started jobs may genuinely terminate later, but a cancelled record
alone cannot substantiate that process-exit claim at this adapter boundary.
Their status remains indeterminate pending execution/termination evidence,
instead of being misclassified as pre-start revocation. Enqueue cleanup must
not promote that uncertain response to durableCancellationConfirmed.

Twelve actual pre-repair failures are retained in
`production-low-resource-deepseek-stop-state-red-20261002-20261001.log`.
Fourteen cases cover reference and owner-discovery paths, valid revocation,
wrong/missing runtime, remaining digest/expiry, start intent and actual start.
Valid context/identity fixtures and the enqueue fake now retain the runtime ID
provided by their approved task, without removing existing safety assertions.
All eight suites passed in
`production-low-resource-deepseek-stop-state-green-20261002-20261001.log`.
All 89 CI contract tests and scoped whitespace checks passed separately.

## Dashboard decision freshness and selection races repaired (October 2)

Source inspection found that resolveDashboardDecision reused the old dashboard

read and ordinary pursuit-detail read could also overwrite each other's selected
context, and read errors displayed raw server text. Four actual component-logic
regressions failed before repair: missing decision, navigation during a pending
decision read, an obsolete pursuit-detail response, and raw error disclosure.

found in its current queue. It never substitutes the old card. The two selection
read paths cancel one another before starting their replacement; a single-response
subscription prevents repeated dispatch. Raw read errors use fixed recovery copy.
An additional failing regression reproduced loading stuck after empty completion;
the repaired completion handler releases loading without executing a decision.
Another regression confirms no dispatch after component destruction. All six
groups are included in the existing frontend test script, not a detached suite.

## Three pursuit decision mutation response paths consolidated (October 2)

The next-action, runtime-recovery-review and completion-review callbacks each
unconditionally adopted the returned detail, claimed workflow/completion success
and displayed raw mutation error text. A component regression first reproduced
a foreign response replacing the selected pursuit. Their shared observer now
captures the request pursuit and decision IDs, requires the exact pursuit and a
well-formed decision queue without the original decision, and adopts detail only
when the current selection still matches. Success wording describes the returned
decision record, not worker execution, created workflow or verified completion.
Missing, malformed, still-present, empty and failed responses use fixed uncertainty
copy instructing review before repeat, without raw provider details.

The observer consumes one response and finalizes the busy flag on completion,
error or destruction. Its subscription is retained for component cleanup; ordinary
navigation does not automatically cancel a mutation. Unsubscribing on destruction
only ends UI observation and is NOT proof that a dispatched server effect was
cancelled or absent. Durable server idempotency/reconciliation remains required.

Two new regression groups cover eight response modes across all three actual
mutation methods (24 variants), plus single-response and destruction behavior.

## Raw decision response validation and candidate mutation handling repaired (October 2)
