validate the raw pursuit ID and explicit well-formed queue before normalization.
Invalid responses fail with a fixed error; the raw object is not altered. Ordinary
read-view normalization remains unchanged. A combined actual-service/component
test confirms an absent queue cannot become a successful decision acknowledgement.

Candidate acceptance now uses the existing checked decision observer, preserving
newer selection and uncertainty on missing/still-present decision evidence.
Candidate archival checks the returned pursuit ID, archived=true and exact
archived status before success, clears inspectors only for its original selected
pursuit, and retains a newer selection. Errors/empty responses are unconfirmed,
never automatic retries. Both paths own their single-response observer and clean
up on destruction without claiming that server-side effects were cancelled.
A candidate regression failed before repair on a foreign detail replacing the
selection. Twelve variants cover both approval choices and six response outcomes;
two destruction cases additionally verify no post-destruction UI updates.

coverage gap. This client consistency gate is not server authorization and cannot
prevent another operator from changing the record after the request is sent.
Transactional pending-decision enforcement and live authenticated tests remain
required on the backend.

## Standalone pursuit archive/reopen lifecycle handling repaired (October 2)

state on reopening. Errors and empty/mismatched responses remain unconfirmed.
The buttons show their in-flight state and are disabled during conflicting detail,
lifecycle or decision operations. Public/dashboard decision entrypoints also
refuse dispatch during a pending lifecycle operation.

Verified archive clears only its own selected/requested pursuit, cancels its
pending detail/decision/evidence reads and closes its inspectors. Another selected
or loading pursuit is preserved. Verified reopen adopts the returned record and
refreshes detail only when the original pursuit remains selected. No response
implies task execution or automatically retries a failed mutation. Destruction
ends UI observation, not proof that a dispatched server mutation was cancelled.

## Ambient proposal decision lifecycle (October 2)

- Reproduced double accept/dismiss dispatch in the actual Ambient Brain component before repair. Decisions now require a current proposed record with matching need/source identity, no conflicting refresh or scan, and no unresolved decision-state uncertainty.
- Added tracked single-response, bounded-time decision and overview subscriptions, finalized loading states, destruction cancellation, and fixed non-secret error/empty-response handling. Uncertainty requires a successful explicit overview read before further decisions; unrelated autonomy-telemetry errors do not revoke otherwise current proposal decisions.

## Ambient owner-priority save acknowledgement (October 2)

- Reproduced a foreign profile response closing the current need editor before repair. Response ID/key, submitted numbers, enabled flag and trimmed notes must match before the displayed baseline changes.
- Newer overview snapshots and selected profile editors are preserved. A reopened editor or edited draft is not closed by an older response. Successful acknowledgement is labelled as returned profile values, not independently durable persistence or progress in personal wellbeing.
- Refresh, scan, decision and profile-save controls now share their competing-operation guards rather than looking clickable while the handler refuses them. No implicit scan is started by a preference write.

Default need scores in backend seed code are configuration, not measured human outcomes. No agents, containers, builds, downloads, restarts, commits or real profile writes occurred.

## Ambient scan acknowledgement and status recovery (October 2)

- Scan requests now consume one bounded response and cancel observation on component destruction. Running, failed, empty, reused, wrong-trigger, malformed timestamp and invalid-counter responses cannot become completed scan notifications. Only a new completed manual scan record with valid identifiers, timestamps and nonnegative integer counters is acknowledged; reported proposal counts are explicitly not task-execution proof.
- Uncertain scan outcomes require status recovery before another pass. Overview reads require explicit opportunity, need and scan arrays and valid scan status/start-time records; malformed reads retain previous records. Pending or reported-running scans prevent duplicate dispatch. Failed and empty overview reads also block scanning instead of silently re-enabling it.
- Displayed completed scan counts require a valid completed record. Invalid running counts are unavailable, not invented zeroes. Refreshing status does not launch a scan.
- Regression evidence: the new recovery test initially failed because an empty overview allowed another scan (20/21 passed; failing run waited for the outstanding timeout). The guard was repaired and fixture cleanup moved into a finally block. The focused actual-component suite then passed 21/21 in under two seconds; the serial broader native suite passed 126/126. The CI contract suite passed 89/89; three dashboard Angular template/TypeScript roots and five spec TypeScript roots passed under the existing 384 MB limit.
- Scope: actual component/RxJS behavior and compiler consistency only. No rendered browser acceptance, live provider/source calls, independent durable database readback, request-specific scan receipt or server concurrency/recovery proof was performed. HAI remained stopped; Joyce and ShareT were left untouched. No agents, containers, downloads, production build, deployments, commits or pushes were started.

## Ambient telemetry admission and stale presentation (October 2)

No equality is inferred between aggregate totals and the separately truncated recent evaluation list.

## IDP initial pool acquisition and resource bounds (October 2)

- Reviewed the remaining opening path: gorm.Open originally performed an automatic initial ping with a newly allocated pool, but NewPostgresDatabase returned an error without closing that pool. A synthetic-driver regression exercises actual GORM behavior: failed automatic ping returns an error/handle, invokes no driver close, and the caller's pool is still usable when the fixture failure is cleared. This reproduces the library ownership behavior underlying the source finding; no actual PostgreSQL connection was attempted.
- NewPostgresDatabase retains its public signature and delegates to NewPostgresDatabaseContext. Nil/already-cancelled contexts fail before connection setup. Encoded configuration uses the existing pgx stdlib connector wrapped with a whole-acquisition five-second context deadline, so earlier parent deadlines remain effective instead of relying only on per-host driver timeout.
- initializeIDPPostgresPool explicitly owns the SQL pool, disables GORM automatic ping, and performs the initial ping with a ten-second child context. Failure, cancellation (including a fixture that returns nil after cancelling its parent), or panic before ownership transfer closes the pool; returned errors preserve original and cleanup causes. Successful handles keep a neutral GORM context rather than the expired opening context.
- Each IDP pool now has fixed caps of eight open/two idle connections, a two-minute idle lifetime and a thirty-minute connection lifetime. These are separate from backend DB_* configuration, per pool rather than installation-wide, and not measured CPU/RAM savings. README distinguishes those scopes and still-open migration/TLS/live acceptance requirements.
- Native offline regressions passed for failed ping, cleanup error, pre-cancelled/nil context, deadline expiry during ping, late cancellation despite nil driver outcome, successful pool identity/context/limits and earlier connector deadline. An additional actual database/sql fixture test holds all eight allowed connections, rejects a ninth through caller deadline, then confirms two idle connections retained and six underlying connections closed. Fixtures use no network/database server and do not establish real DNS/driver timeout or concurrent PostgreSQL acceptance.
- Existing startup ownership, credential-field and first-run password cases passed in the same selected package runs with standard vet, serial execution and a 384 MiB memory target. Full native Windows IDP compile-only pass covered all 17 packages; retained logs under hai-idp-bounded-pool-compile-d5030901526549538186825e165d552d. The subsequent extra capacity regression passed in its actual package run; no production source changed after the whole-IDP compile. All 92 Python CI/configuration source contracts passed again.

## IDP database startup context and step-boundary cancellation (October 2)

- Audited the remaining IDP database initialization sequence: its new connection deadlines did not cover subsequent migration/seed SQL. Added a cancellation regression for before migration, after migration and after seed. Initial fixture compilation failed due to a missing GORM import, corrected before behavioral testing. All three behavioral cases then failed against the existing sequence because it accepted cancellation as successful startup and kept the pool.
- GetDefaultDB now delegates to GetDefaultDBContext. idpStartupContext validates the parent and IDP_DB_STARTUP_TIMEOUT before connection setup, defaults to five minutes and honors an earlier parent deadline. NewPostgresDatabaseContext receives that context; migration and seed receive a contextual clone of the same owned database. Successful startup returns the original neutral runtime handle, not the context cancelled when startup returns.
- prepareDefaultDatabase checks its bound context before migration, before seed and before ownership transfer. Cancelled startup returns the context error and closes its owned pool rather than seeding after cancellation or reporting success. These checks do not roll back committed migration/seed writes or remove partially created records.

No actual database or provider was contacted.

All 93 Python contracts passed; that is source wiring proof, not a deployed-container configuration check.

## IDP route-owned client cleanup (October 2)

- Added a small serial-startup OwnedResources collector, closing io.Closer resources in reverse acquisition order, retaining every close error, and using sync.Once to prevent repeated/concurrent close calls. It is not a concurrent registration API; resources are registered during startup before serving.
- Default auth and user-service builders track only their internally created resources, not the borrowed SQL database. Auth owns its optional logger, optional sender and revocation Redis client; the user service owns its separate optional logger. Partial construction or panic runs deferred cleanup; successful construction transfers the owner to the service. Services built with supplied dependencies via NewService/NewUserService do not acquire ownership of those dependencies.
- The handler owns only its reset limiter. The limiter tracks its lazy Redis client, with nil/no-client cleanup safe. Handler.Close does not close the borrowed auth service. Actual route construction registers auth service, handler and user service immediately after creation; Initialize joins their cleanup with pool.Close, in that order, when route setup or the HTTP serve call returns. Source contract was updated to verify that wiring.
- Four new cleanup tests passed: reverse order/error preservation, concurrent once-only cleanup, auth-owned resources and handler-limiter separation. The handler test verifies redis.ErrClosed on the already-closed lazy client without opening a Redis connection. Together with existing router/limiter cases, 22 selected native test functions passed across services/authentication/router. ## IDP HTTP recovery and access-error privacy (October 2)

- Inspected the actual cached Gin v1.9.1 recovery implementation: it logs panic text/stack, dumps request headers in debug mode, and masks Authorization but not cookies. Broken connections attach the raw error to c.Errors. The IDP previously used gin.Recovery, and its access formatter suppressed error payloads only on token-bearing reset paths.

The nil output writer avoids the raw request dump, panic formatting and source-file stack reads while preserving Gin's recovery/broken-connection detection. ## IDP authentication duration overflow validation (October 3)

- Source audit found authenticationIntSetting accepted positive integers without checking subsequent unit multiplication. Access/reset durations multiply at use, login spacing/blocking multiply at use, and refresh days multiplied during configuration. Large representable integers could therefore overflow time.Duration and alter expiry/blocking behavior.
- Added range validation for all five time settings against MaxInt64 divided by the actual unit (minutes, seconds, hours or 24-hour days). Existing scalar/count fields retain their units for callers. Refresh conversion now multiplies time.Duration(days) by the already-bounded day unit, removing the intermediate int multiplication. Max-attempt counts are not durations and remain subject to their existing integer/positivity checks.
- Added one native test function with five setting subcases: largest representable duration is accepted and preserved; the next value is rejected with the setting name but not the supplied value. The full native config package test suite passed. Evidence: C:\Users\NO\AppData\Local\Temp\hai-idp-duration-bounds-9bef211aa3d343328199eaf62377aa57.

## IDP strict optional-feature boolean validation (October 3)

- Source audit found getEnvBool silently returned its default for explicitly malformed values. Kafka enablement and local login bypass therefore silently became disabled; SMTP STARTTLS silently reverted to true. Safe defaults are retained, but operator mistakes were not reported.
- getEnvBool now returns a value/error, trims surrounding whitespace and uses standard strconv.ParseBool spelling. Missing values retain the supplied default; explicitly blank/malformed values return a fixed setting-name error without echoing input. Wired error propagation through Kafka, local preview and mail constructors. newMailConfig now returns an error and Setup propagates it before proceeding.
- Three new native test functions passed alongside the full config test suite: missing true/false defaults and six valid spellings; twelve invalid constructor cases across the three settings; optional unconfigured mail with explicit true/false TLS choices. Evidence: C:\Users\NO\AppData\Local\Temp\hai-idp-boolean-config-bf916de06f8a4987b5dcaf035e8cc2ad.
- All 17 IDP packages compiled with no test functions selected (ten test packages/seven without tests), and all 96 CI contracts passed. Evidence: C:\Users\NO\AppData\Local\Temp\hai-idp-boolean-compile-107811bc4288469db53df9bab83cf5fe. Scoped whitespace checks passed.
- This verifies parser/constructor behavior and source startup propagation, not live operator environment, SMTP transport security, login-bypass exposure or optional Kafka availability. No runtime settings were changed. No HAI startup, agents, containers, downloads, databases, migrations, external calls, commits or pushes; Joyce and ShareT remain untouched.

## IDP HTTP startup configuration validation (October 3)

- Validated `WEB_SERVER_PORT` before infrastructure creation: range 1 through 65535; normalized accepted numeric input before binding. Port zero no longer silently selects an undiscoverable ephemeral listener.
- Validated `BASE_URL` as a static absolute route prefix. Rejects dynamic Gin parameters, URL query/fragment/escape syntax, backslashes, control characters, and ambiguous dot or repeated-slash segments. Empty and root prefixes remain supported; trailing slash is normalized.
- Configuration diagnostics identify the setting without echoing its raw value.
- Native offline configuration suite passed, including three new test functions for defaults, normalization, and invalid/private values. Evidence: `%TEMP%/hai-idp-server-config-cff6df629d0b478c8841f6fc12548f8f`.
- All 17 IDP packages passed native compile-only verification (10 test packages with no test functions selected, 7 without test files). Evidence: `%TEMP%/hai-idp-server-compile-cda9235bbbb741269d19455b6afef6cb`. All 96 CI-contract checks and scoped whitespace validation also passed.
- This is configuration-unit evidence, not a real listener, gateway, database, or browser-login acceptance result. No containers, agents, downloads, migrations, commits, or deployments were started.

## IDP SMTP credential fidelity (October 3)

- Fixed two independent password alterations: mail configuration and the SMTP sender constructor both trimmed credential whitespace. Both now preserve the supplied password exactly; ordinary host/port/mailbox normalization is unchanged.
- Two new offline regressions cover leading/trailing whitespace, tabs/newline and empty or space-only values without printing credentials. The sender still rejects an absent password when a username is supplied and still requires encrypted delivery.
- Nine selected offline test functions passed across configuration and SMTP services, including recipient rejection before connection, incomplete settings, encryption policy, envelope/header parsing and redacted delivery diagnostics. No network listener or provider was involved. Evidence: `%TEMP%/hai-idp-smtp-password-70fac11770e447a18ba7d12bd7dfaa0c`.

## IDP SMTP transport-port consistency (October 3)

- The SMTP readiness check accepted integer spellings such as `+465`, while the connection used the unnormalized spelling. The sender now canonicalizes every accepted port to decimal digits before constructing the transport address. Invalid values stay disabled rather than falling back to a default port.
- A new offline regression covers five accepted spellings/boundaries and six invalid values, checks the exact transport endpoint, and preserves the implicit-TLS-versus-STARTTLS selection. No dial, listener, email or provider operation was performed.
- Nine selected SMTP service test functions passed: `%TEMP%/hai-idp-smtp-port-tests-71ebae6fcbe0415c99dbfb6456b95909`. All 96 CI-contract checks passed.
- All 17 IDP packages passed compile-only verification: `%TEMP%/hai-idp-smtp-port-compile-261e23e2c8dc406b91300cb76b977e3d`. Scoped whitespace validation passed.
- This establishes local configuration consistency only, not SMTP provider authentication, remote certificate policy or real reset-email receipt. HAI remains stopped; Joyce and ShareT were not changed.

## IDP recovery-service backend-error log privacy (October 3)

- New synthetic regression demonstrated raw backend-error disclosure in all four recovery-service paths: storing a reset digest, fetching by reset token, consuming a reset token, and clearing an undelivered token. Baseline failed all four subcases: `%TEMP%/hai-reset-log-baseline-a8631f993785475584180b0f8c7ab83d`.
- Replaced raw error formatting at those four service log sites with fixed stage-specific diagnostics; token-generation logging uses a fixed diagnostic too. Operational failures and existing public error classification remain intact. SMTP stage allowlisting remains unchanged.
- Eighteen selected offline authentication test functions passed, including the new four-subcase privacy test and existing reset HTTP/service behavior: `%TEMP%/hai-reset-log-tests-dc7cb0669011464ea62c43326216f918`.
- These tests use fake user services, captured logging and in-memory HTTP requests; they do not prove real database concurrency, rollback, token persistence or SMTP delivery. They establish privacy at the audited service log sites, not every lower-layer or application log.
- No agents, containers, downloads, migrations, commits or deployments were started. HAI stays stopped and Joyce/ShareT remain untouched.
